may have projections on span
\{\Psi_{j}\}
, we further decompose
\rho_{**,0}=\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\alpha_{j,0}^{**}\Psi_{j}+\rho_{**,0}^{\perp}
,
where
\{\alpha_{j,0}^{**}\}
is chosen such that

\displaystyle\left\langle\rho_{**,0}^{\perp},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle=\left\langle\rho_{**,0}^{\perp},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle=\left\langle\rho_{**,0}^{\perp},w_{j,l}\right\rangle=0

for all
1\leq j\leq\nu
and all
1\leq l\leq d
. On the other hand, by the orthogonal conditions of
\rho_{*}
given in (
3.16
) and Lemmas
4.4
,
4.5
and
4.6
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\Psi_{l},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle\alpha_{l,0}^{**}=-\left\langle\gamma_{1,ex},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle-\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\gamma_{1,i},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle-\left\langle\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,*},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle

(4.38)

for all
1\leq j\leq\nu
. Since
\Psi
is a solution of (
2.5
), by Lemmas
4.3
,
4.4
,
4.5
and
4.7
,

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\left|\left\langle\gamma_{1,ex},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle\right|=\left|\left\langle\gamma_{1,ex},\Psi_{j}^{p}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|\lesssim Q,\\
&\left|\left\langle\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\gamma_{1,i},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle\right|=\left|\left\langle\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\gamma_{1,i},\Psi_{j}^{p}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\left|\left(\alpha_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-1}-1\right|,\\
&\left|\left\langle\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,*},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle\right|=\left|\left\langle\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,*},\Psi_{j}^{p}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|\lesssim\beta_{*}^{2}.\end{aligned}\right.

Intersecting these estimates into (
4.38
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left|\alpha_{j,0}^{**}\right|\lesssim Q+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left|\left(\alpha_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-1}-1\right|+\beta_{*}^{2}.

(4.39)

Moreover, by (
3.16
), (
4.18
), (
4.25
) and (
4.32
),
\rho_{**,0}^{\perp}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{L}(\rho_{**,0}^{\perp})=f+\mathcal{R}_{new},\quad\text{in }\mathcal{C},\\
&\langle\Psi_{j},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\rangle=\langle\partial_{t}\Psi_{j},\rho_{**,0}^{\perp}\rangle=\langle w_{j,l},\rho_{**,0}^{\perp}\rangle=0\quad\text{for }1\leq j\leq\nu\text{ and }1\leq l\leq d,\end{aligned}\right.

(4.40)

where by (
3.16
) and Lemmas
4.2
,
4.4

4.5
,
4.6
and
4.7
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{new}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\Psi_{i}^{p-1}\left((c_{1,ex,i}+c_{1,j,i}+c_{3,led,i})\partial_{t}\Psi_{i}+\sum_{l=1}^{d}(\varsigma_{2,ex,i,l}+\varsigma_{3,led,i,l})w_{i,l}\right)

(4.41)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left(2A_{p}\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}+3B_{p}\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\right)\rho_{*}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}}+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{R}_{2,j}+\mathcal{N}_{rem}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}2A_{p}\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\mathcal{V}-\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}\right)\rho_{*}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}^{2}\mathcal{U}_{j}\Psi_{j}^{2p-3}\left(\Psi_{j}+\rho_{*}\right)\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}}+2A_{p}\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\mathcal{V}\rho_{*}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\cup_{j=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{B}_{j}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\alpha_{j,0}^{**}\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-1}-\Psi_{j}^{p-1}\right)\Psi_{j},

where
A_{p}
and
B_{p}
are given in Lemma
4.1
.
Even though we have picked up all possible leading order terms of
\rho_{*}
in terms of
Q
,
\beta_{*}
and
\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left|(\alpha_{j}^{*})^{p-1}-1\right|
, the data
\mathcal{R}_{new}
, given by (
4.41
), is not good enough to control
\rho_{**,0}^{\perp}
in a desired size. This is mainly because the optimal Bianchi-Egnell stability of the CKN inequality for
d\geq 2
,
a<0
and
b=b_{FS}(a)
is quartic, as shown in
[
53
, Theorem 1]
, which implies that we only have the opportunity to control the terms of order
\beta_{*}^{4}
from above. Thus, we need to ensure that the (possible) singular part should be smaller than
\beta_{*}^{4}
. Keep this in mind, we need to eliminate the lower order terms (compared to the
\beta_{*}^{4}
terms) in the data
\mathcal{R}_{new}
. For this purpose, we need the following.

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
a<0
and
b=b_{FS}(a)
. Then we can decompose

\displaystyle\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,*}=\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,rem,j,*},

where
\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j}
is even in terms of
t-s_{j}^{*}
and satisfies the equation

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{L}(\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j})=\mathcal{N}_{led,j}-\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\Psi_{i}^{p-1}\left(c_{\mathcal{N},led,j,i}\partial_{t}\Psi_{i}+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\varsigma_{\mathcal{N},led,j,i,l}w_{i,l}\right),\quad\text{in }\mathcal{C},\\
&\langle\partial_{t}\Psi_{i},\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j}\rangle=\langle w_{i,l},\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j}\rangle=0\quad\text{for all }1\leq i\leq\nu\text{ and all }1\leq l\leq d,\end{aligned}\right.

with
\mathcal{N}_{led,j}=\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}+\mathcal{V}_{j}\right)
and

\displaystyle\beta_{*}^{2}\gtrsim\sup_{(t,\theta)\in\mathcal{C}}\frac{|\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j}|}{\Psi_{j}^{1-\sigma}(t)}+\sup_{(t,\theta)\in\mathcal{C}}\frac{|\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,rem,j,*}|}{\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\Psi_{i}^{1-\sigma}(t)}.

Moreover, we can decompose
\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j}=\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j,*}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j,**}
with
\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j,*}
being even on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,
\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j,**}
being odd on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
and

\displaystyle 1\gtrsim\sup_{(t,\theta)\in\mathcal{C}}\frac{|\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j,*}|}{\beta_{*}^{2}\Psi_{j}^{1-\sigma}(t)}+\sup_{(t,\theta)\in\mathcal{C}}\frac{|\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j,**}|}{\beta_{*}^{3}\Psi_{i}^{1-\sigma}(t)},

where
\sigma>0
is chosen to satisfy (
4.27
).

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Lemma
4.7
so we omit it. Moreover, since
\mathcal{N}_{led,j}
is even in terms of
t-s_{j}^{*}
, by uniqueness, we also have that
\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j}
is even in terms of
t-s_{j}^{*}
. On the other hand, the decomposition of
\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j}
is generated by the data
\mathcal{N}_{led,j}=\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}+\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{3}
. The first part
\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j,*}
is obtained by the data
\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}
which is even on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
while, the second part
\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j,**}
is obtained by the data
\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{3}
which is odd on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
.
∎

By Lemma
4.8
, we can consider the following equation:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{L}(\rho_{**,1,j}^{\perp})=\mathcal{R}_{new,*,j}+\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\Psi_{i}^{p-1}\left(c_{new,*,j,i}\partial_{t}\Psi_{i}+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\varsigma_{new,*,j,i,l}w_{i,l}\right),\quad\text{in }\mathcal{C},\\
&\langle\partial_{t}\Psi_{j},\rho_{**,1}^{\perp}\rangle=\langle w_{j,l},\rho_{**,1}^{\perp}\rangle=0\quad\text{for }1\leq j\leq\nu\text{ and }1\leq l\leq d,\end{aligned}\right.

(4.42)

where
\mathcal{R}_{new,*,j}=2A_{p}\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j}
with
A_{p}
given in Lemma
4.1
.

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
a<0
and
b=b_{FS}(a)
. Then (
4.42
) is uniquely solvable. Moreover, the solution
\rho_{**,1,j}^{\perp}
is even in terms of
t-s_{j}^{*}
and satisfies

\displaystyle\beta_{*}^{3}\gtrsim\sup_{(t,\theta)\in\mathcal{C}}\frac{|\rho_{**,1,j}^{\perp}|}{\Psi_{j}^{1-\sigma}(t)},

where the Lagrange multipliers
\{c_{new,*,j,i}\}
and
\{\varsigma_{new,*,j,i,l}\}
are chosen such that the right hand side of the equation (
4.42
) is orthogonal to
\{\Psi_{j}^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\}
and
\{\Psi_{j}^{p-1}w_{j,l}\}
in
L^{2}(\mathcal{C})
and
\sigma>0
is chosen to satisfy (
4.27
).
The Lagrange multipliers also satisfy
|c_{new,*,j,i}|\lesssim\beta_{*}^{3}Q^{p}
and
\left|\varsigma_{new,*,j,i,l}\right|\lesssim\beta_{*}^{3}

for all
1\leq i,j\leq\nu
and all
1\leq l\leq d
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Lemma
4.7
so we omit it. Moreover, since
\mathcal{R}_{new,*,j}
is even in terms of
t-s_{j}^{*}
by Lemma
4.8
, by uniqueness, we also have that
\rho_{**,1,j}^{\perp}
is even in terms of
t-s_{j}^{*}
. Moreover, similar to that of (
4.36
) and (
4.37
), by the oddness of
\partial_{t}\Psi
in
\mathbb{R}
, we also have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}|c_{new,*,j,i}|\lesssim\beta_{*}^{3}Q^{p}\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}|\varsigma_{new,*,j,i,l}|\lesssim\beta_{*}^{3}

for all
1\leq j\leq\nu
.
∎

Let

\displaystyle\rho_{**,1}^{\perp}=\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\rho_{**,1,j}^{\perp}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{R}_{new,*}=\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{R}_{new,*,j}.

Clearly,
\rho_{**,1}^{\perp}
may also have projections on
span\{\Psi_{l}\}
. Thus, as above, we decompose

\displaystyle\rho_{**,1}^{\perp}=\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}\alpha_{l,1}^{**}\Psi_{l}+\rho_{**,2}^{\perp},

where
\{\alpha_{l,1}^{**}\}
is chosen such that

\displaystyle\left\langle\rho_{**,2}^{\perp},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle=\left\langle\rho_{**,2}^{\perp},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle=\left\langle\rho_{**,2}^{\perp},w_{j,l}\right\rangle=0.

(4.43)

Moreover, by (
4.42
), we know that
\rho_{**,2}^{\perp}
satisfies the following equation:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{L}(\rho_{**,2}^{\perp})=\mathcal{R}_{new,**}+\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\Psi_{i}^{p-1}\left(c_{new,*,i}\partial_{t}\Psi_{i}+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\varsigma_{new,*,i,l}w_{i,l}\right),\quad\text{in }\mathcal{C},\\
&\left\langle\rho_{**,2}^{\perp},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle=\langle\partial_{t}\Psi_{j},\rho_{**,2}^{\perp}\rangle=\langle w_{j,l},\rho_{**,2}^{\perp}\rangle=0\quad\text{for }1\leq j\leq\nu\text{ and }1\leq l\leq d,\end{aligned}\right.

(4.44)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{new,**}=\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}2A_{p}\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j}+\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}p\alpha_{l,1}^{**}\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-1}-\Psi_{l}^{p-1}\right)\Psi_{l},

(4.45)

with
A_{p}
given by Lemma
4.1
.

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
a<0
and
b=b_{FS}(a)
. Then we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left|\alpha_{j,1}^{**}\right|\lesssim\beta_{*}^{4}+\beta_{*}^{3}Q

(4.46)

and

\displaystyle\beta_{*}^{4}+\beta_{*}^{3}Q\gtrsim\left\{\begin{aligned} &\|\rho_{**,2}^{\perp}\|_{\sharp}+\sup_{(t,\theta)\in\mathcal{C}}\frac{|\rho_{**,2}^{\perp}|}{\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\Psi_{j}^{1-\sigma}(t)},\quad p\geq 3,\\
&\|\rho_{**,2}^{\perp}\|_{\natural,1,*}+\sup_{(t,\theta)\in\mathcal{C}}\frac{|\rho_{**,2}^{\perp}|}{\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\Psi_{j}^{1-\sigma}(t)},\quad 1<p<3,\end{aligned}\right.

(4.47)

where
\|\cdot\|_{\sharp}
and
\|\cdot\|_{\natural,1,*}
are given in Lemma
4.4
and
\sigma>0
is chosen to satisfy (
4.27
).

Proof.

By (
4.42
) and (
4.43
),

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\Psi_{l},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle\alpha_{l,1}^{**}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{new,*},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+p\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-1}\rho_{**,1}^{\perp},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

(4.48)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}p\alpha_{l,1}^{**}\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-1}\Psi_{l},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+p\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-1}\rho_{**,2}^{\perp},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{new,*},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

for all
1\leq j\leq\nu
. By the oddness of
\{\mathcal{V}_{j}\}
on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
and Lemmas
4.3
and
4.8
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{new,*},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\beta_{*}^{4}+\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\beta_{*}^{3}\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{\frac{3p-1-2\sigma}{2}},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}\alpha_{l,1}^{**}\left\langle\Psi_{l}^{p-1},\mathcal{U}_{l}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\beta_{*}^{4}+\beta_{*}^{3}Q+\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}Q\alpha_{l,1}^{**}.

On the other hand, by Lemma
4.3
and similar estimates of (
4.30
),

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}p\alpha_{l,1}^{**}\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-1}\Psi_{l},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}p\alpha_{l,1}^{**}\left\langle\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-1}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}}\Psi_{l},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}p\alpha_{l,1}^{**}\left\langle\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-1}-\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-1}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}}\right)\Psi_{l},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}p\alpha_{l,1}^{**}\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-1}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\cup_{i=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{B}_{i}}\Psi_{l},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p\|\Psi\|^{2}\alpha_{j,1}^{**}+\sum_{l=1;l\not=j}^{\nu}\mathcal{O}(Q)\alpha_{l,1}^{**}

and further by (
4.43
), we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-1}\rho_{**,2}^{\perp},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-1}-\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-1}\right)\Psi_{j},\rho_{**,2}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\rho_{**,2}^{\perp}\|\times\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{O}\left(Q\right),\quad p>2,\\
&\mathcal{O}\left(Q\left|\log Q\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}\right),\quad p=2,\\
&\mathcal{O}\left(Q^{\frac{p}{2}}\right),\quad 1<p<2.\end{aligned}\right.

It follows from (
4.48
) that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left|\alpha_{j,1}^{**}\right|\lesssim\beta_{*}^{4}+\beta_{*}^{3}Q+Q^{\frac{p}{2}\wedge 1}\left|\log Q\right|\|\rho_{**,2}^{\perp}\|.

Now, by multiplying (
4.44
) with
\rho_{**,2}^{\perp}
on both sides and integrating by parts, we have
\|\rho_{**,2}^{\perp}\|\lesssim\beta_{*}^{4}+\beta_{*}^{3}Q
, which implies that (
4.46
) holds true. To obtain the estimate (
4.47
), we shall decompose
\mathcal{R}_{new,**}
into two parts, where
\mathcal{R}_{new,**}
is given by (
4.45
). The first part is given by
\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}p(p-1)\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j}
, which generates the bound

\displaystyle\sup_{(t,\theta)\in\mathcal{C}}\frac{|\rho_{**,2}^{\perp}|}{\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\Psi_{j}^{1-\sigma}(t)}\lesssim\beta_{*}^{4}+\beta_{*}^{3}Q

as that of
\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}
. The second part is given by
\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}p\alpha_{l,1}^{**}\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-1}-\Psi_{l}^{p-1}\right)\Psi_{l}
, which generates the bound

\displaystyle\beta_{*}^{4}+\beta_{*}^{3}Q\gtrsim\left\{\begin{aligned} &\|\rho_{**,2}^{\perp}\|_{\sharp},\quad p\geq 3,\\
&\|\rho_{**,2}^{\perp}\|_{\natural,1,*},\quad 1<p<3,\end{aligned}\right.

as that of
\gamma_{1,ex}
.
∎

We also need to consider the following equation:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{L}(\rho_{**,3}^{\perp})=\mathcal{R}_{3,ex}-\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\Psi_{j}^{p-1}\left(c_{3,ex,j}\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\varsigma_{3,ex,j,l}w_{j,l}\right),\quad\text{in }\mathcal{C},\\
&\langle\partial_{t}\Psi_{j},\rho_{**,3}^{\perp}\rangle=\langle w_{j,l},\rho_{**,3}^{\perp}\rangle=0\quad\text{for all }1\leq j\leq\nu\text{ and all }1\leq l\leq d,\end{aligned}\right.

(4.49)

where
\mathcal{R}_{3,ex}=2A_{p}\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\mathcal{V}\gamma_{1,ex}
.

Lemma 4.11
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
a<0
and
b=b_{FS}(a)
. Then (
4.49
) is uniquely solvable. Moreover, the solution
\rho_{**,3}^{\perp}
is odd on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
and satisfies

\displaystyle\beta_{*}\gtrsim\left\{\begin{aligned} &\|\rho_{**,3}^{\perp}\|_{\sharp},\quad p\geq\frac{7}{3},\\
&\|\rho_{**,3}^{\perp}\|_{\natural,2,*},\quad 1<p<\frac{7}{3},\end{aligned}\right.

(4.50)

where the Lagrange multipliers
\{c_{3,ex,j}\}
and
\{\varsigma_{3,ex,j,l}\}
are chosen such that the right hand side of the equation (
4.49
) is orthogonal to
\{\Psi_{j}^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\}
and
\{\Psi_{j}^{p-1}w_{j,l}\}
in
L^{2}(\mathcal{C})
and
the norms
\|\cdot\|_{\sharp}
and
\|\cdot\|_{\natural,2,*}
are given in Lemma
4.6
.
The Lagrange multipliers also satisfy
c_{3,ex,j}=0
for all
1\leq j\leq\nu
and
|\varsigma_{3,ex,j,l}|\lesssim\beta_{*}Q
for all
1\leq j\leq\nu
and
1\leq l\leq d
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.4
,
\gamma_{1,ex}
is even on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
. Moreover, similar to (
4.30
), direct calculations show that

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{R}_{3,ex}\right|\lesssim\left\{\begin{aligned} &\beta_{*}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\Psi_{i}^{\frac{3p-1-2\sigma}{2}}\left(Q_{i}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i,+}}+Q_{i-1}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i,-}}\right)+Q\mathcal{U}^{\frac{3p-1-2\sigma}{2}}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\left(\cup_{i=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{B}_{i}\right)}\right),\quad p\geq 3,\\
&\beta_{*}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\Psi_{i}^{\frac{5p-7}{2}}\left(Q_{i}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i,+}}+Q_{i-1}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i,-}}\right)+Q\mathcal{U}^{\frac{3p-1-2\sigma}{2}}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\left(\cup_{i=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{B}_{i}\right)}\right),\quad 1<p<3.\end{aligned}\right.

(4.51)

Since
\Psi_{i}^{\frac{5p-7}{2}}\lesssim\Psi_{i}^{\frac{3p-5}{2}}
in
\mathcal{B}_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq\nu
, the rest of the proof is the same of that of Lemma
4.6
, so we omit it here.
∎

Let
\rho_{**}^{\perp}=\rho_{**,0}^{\perp}-\rho_{**,2}^{\perp}-\rho_{**,3}^{\perp}
, then we have the following decomposition of
\rho_{*}
.

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
a<0
and
b=b_{FS}(a)
. Then we have
\rho_{*}=\rho_{0}+\rho_{**}^{\perp}
,
where

(1)

the regular part
\rho_{0}=\gamma_{ex}+\gamma_{*}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}

and

(i)

\gamma_{ex}=\sum_{l=1}^{2}\gamma_{l,ex}
with
\gamma_{1,ex}
even on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
and
\gamma_{2,ex}=\overline{\gamma}_{2,ex}+\rho_{**,3}^{\perp}
odd on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
satisfying

\displaystyle 1\gtrsim\left\{\begin{aligned} &\left\|\gamma_{ex}\right\|_{\sharp}+\left\|\gamma_{1,ex}\right\|_{\sharp},\quad p\geq 3,\\
&\left\|\gamma_{ex}\right\|_{\natural,1,*}+\left\|\gamma_{1,ex}\right\|_{\natural,1,*},\quad 1<p<3\end{aligned}\right.

and

\displaystyle\beta_{*}\gtrsim\left\{\begin{aligned} &\|\gamma_{2,ex}\|_{\sharp},\quad p\geq\frac{7}{3},\\
&\|\gamma_{2,ex}\|_{\natural,2,*},\quad 1<p<\frac{7}{3},\end{aligned}\right.

(ii)

\gamma_{*}=\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\gamma_{j,*}
is even on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
with
\gamma_{j,*}
even in terms of
t-s_{j}^{*}
in
\mathbb{R}
and satisfying

\displaystyle Q+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left|\left(\alpha_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-1}-1\right|+\beta_{*}^{2}\gtrsim\sup_{(t,\theta)\in\mathcal{C}}\frac{|\gamma_{*}|}{\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\Psi_{j}^{1-\sigma}(t)},

where
\gamma_{j,*}=\gamma_{1,j}+\alpha_{j,0}^{**}\Psi_{j}
and
\sigma>0
is chosen to satisfy (
4.27
).

(iii)

\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}=\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,*}+\rho_{**,2}^{\perp}
with the symmetrical part of
\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}
, in terms of
t-s_{j}^{*}
, given by
\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j}+\rho_{**,1,j}^{\perp}-\alpha_{j,1}^{**}\Psi_{j}
and the remaining parts, denoted by

\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,rem,j}
, satisfies the following estimates

\displaystyle\beta_{*}^{2}\gtrsim\sup_{(t,\theta)\in\mathcal{C}}\frac{|\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,rem,j}|}{\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\Psi_{j}^{1-\sigma}(t)}.

Moreover,
\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}
satisfies the following estimates

\displaystyle 1\gtrsim\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{\|\rho_{**,2}^{\perp}\|_{\sharp}}{\beta_{*}^{4}+\beta_{*}^{3}Q}+\sup_{(t,\theta)\in\mathcal{C}}\frac{|\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,*}|}{\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\beta_{*}^{2}\Psi_{j}^{1-\sigma}(t)},\quad p\geq 3,\\
&\frac{\|\rho_{**,2}^{\perp}\|_{\natural,1,*}}{\beta_{*}^{4}+\beta_{*}^{3}Q}+\sup_{(t,\theta)\in\mathcal{C}}\frac{|\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,*}|}{\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\beta_{*}^{2}\Psi_{j}^{1-\sigma}(t)},\quad 1<p<3,\end{aligned}\right.

where
\sigma>0
is chosen to satisfy (
4.27
).

(2)

The singular part
\rho_{**}^{\perp}
satisfies the following equation:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{L}(\rho_{**}^{\perp})=f+\mathcal{R}_{new,0},\quad\text{in }\mathcal{C},\\
&\langle\Psi_{j},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\rangle=\langle\partial_{t}\Psi_{j},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\rangle=\langle w_{j,l},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\rangle=0\quad\text{for }1\leq j\leq\nu\text{ and }1\leq l\leq d,\end{aligned}\right.

(4.52)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{new,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}(c_{1,ex,i}+c_{1,j,i}+c_{3,led,i}-c_{new,*,i})\Psi_{i}^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi_{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{R}_{2,j}

(4.53)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\sum_{l=1}^{d}(\varsigma_{2,ex,i,l}+\varsigma_{3,led,i,l}-\varsigma_{new,*,i,l})\Psi_{i}^{p-1}w_{i,l}+\mathcal{N}_{rem}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left(2A_{p}\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}(\rho_{*}-\gamma_{1,ex}-\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j})+3B_{p}\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\rho_{*}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}^{2}\mathcal{U}_{j}\Psi_{j}^{2p-3}\left(\Psi_{j}+\rho_{*}\right)+\beta_{*}|\rho_{*}-\gamma_{1,ex}|\Psi_{j}^{\frac{3p-5}{2}}\mathcal{U}_{j}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}|\rho_{*}-\gamma_{1,ex}|\mathcal{U}^{\frac{3(p-1)}{2}}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\left(\cup_{j=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{B}_{j}\right)}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\alpha_{j}^{**}\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-1}-\Psi_{j}^{p-1}\right)\Psi_{j}

with
A_{p}
and
B_{p}
given in Lemma
4.1
,
\alpha_{j}^{**}=\alpha_{j,0}^{**}-\alpha_{j,1}^{**}
and

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left|\alpha_{j}^{**}\right|\lesssim Q+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left|\left(\alpha_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-1}-1\right|+\beta_{*}^{2}.

Proof.

Since
\frac{3p-5}{2}>p-2
for
p>1
, by (
4.29
), (
4.31
), (
4.50
) and (
4.51
), we also have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{R}_{2,ex}+\mathcal{R}_{3,ex}\|_{\natural,1,*}\lesssim\beta_{*}\quad\text{and}\quad\|\gamma_{2,ex}\|_{\natural,1,*}\lesssim\beta_{*}.

(4.54)

Thus, the rest proof of
(i)
of
(1)
follows from Lemmas
4.4
,
4.6
and
4.11
. The conclusion of
(ii)
of
(1)
follows from Lemma
4.5
and (
4.39
). The conclusion of
(iii)
of
(1)
follows from Lemmas
4.8
,
4.9
and
4.10
. The conclusion of
(2)
follows from (
4.39
), (
4.40
), (
4.42
) and Lemma
4.10
.
∎

5.
Refined expansion of
\mathcal{N}
and estimate of
\left\{\alpha_{j}^{*}\right\}

As we stated before, inspired by the optimal Bianchi-Egnell stability of the CKN inequality for
d\geq 2
,
a<0
and
b=b_{FS}(a)
proved in
[
53
, Theorem 1]
, we need to eliminate the lower order terms (compared to the
\beta_{*}^{4}
terms) in the data
\mathcal{R}_{new,0}
to get the desired stability inequality. Thus, we need to refine the expansion of
\mathcal{N}
since we have picked up a regular part
\rho_{0}
in the remaining term
\rho_{*}
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
a<0
and
b=b_{FS}(a)
. Then the nonlinear part
\mathcal{N}
, which is given by (
3.20
), can be refinedly expanded as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{p}\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\left(\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{2}+2\overline{\mathcal{V}}\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right)+B_{p}\mathcal{U}^{p-3}\left(\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{3}+3\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{2}\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right)+\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{rem}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{p}\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\left(\mathcal{V}^{2}+2\mathcal{V}\rho_{*}+\rho_{0}^{2}+2\rho_{0}\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right)+\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{rem}

\displaystyle+B_{p}\mathcal{U}^{p-3}\left(\mathcal{V}^{3}+3\mathcal{V}^{2}\rho_{*}+3\mathcal{V}\rho_{0}^{2}+\rho_{0}^{3}+6\mathcal{V}\rho_{0}\rho_{**}^{\perp}+3\rho_{0}^{2}\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{*}+\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{rem}+A_{p}\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\left(\rho_{0}^{2}+2\rho_{0}\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right)

\displaystyle+B_{p}\mathcal{U}^{p-3}\left(3\mathcal{V}\rho_{0}^{2}+\rho_{0}^{3}+6\mathcal{V}\rho_{0}\rho_{**}^{\perp}+3\rho_{0}^{2}\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{*}+\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{rem}+\mathcal{N}_{0}

in
\mathcal{C}
, where
\mathcal{N}_{*}
is given in (
4.1
),
A_{p}
and
B_{p}
are given in Lemma
4.1
,
\overline{\mathcal{V}}=\mathcal{V}+\rho_{0}
and

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{rem}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-4\sigma}\left(\beta_{*}+Q^{\frac{2\wedge p}{2}}\left|\log Q\right|+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left|\left(\alpha_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-1}-1\right|\right)^{4}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\left(\chi_{p\geq 2}\left|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right|^{2}+\left|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right|^{p}+\left|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right|^{1+\varepsilon}+|\gamma_{*}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}|^{1+\varepsilon}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{*}}\right)

where
\varepsilon>0
is a constant which depends on
p>1
and
\sigma
and
\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{*}
are given by (
4.27
).

Proof.

We improve the set
\mathcal{A}
, used in the proof of Lemma
4.1
, by introducing the set

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{*}=\left\{(\theta,t)\in\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{*}\mid|\rho_{**}^{\perp}|\leq\left|\mathcal{V}+\rho_{0}\right|\right\},

where
\rho_{0}
is the regular part of
\rho_{*}
given in
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
. By
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
, we have

\displaystyle\|\gamma_{ex}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\gamma_{*}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}\|_{L^{\infty}}=o(1),

(5.1)

thus,
\left\|\overline{\mathcal{V}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{C})}\to 0
as
\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\to 0
.
Now, as that of (
4.3
), by the choice of
\sigma
given by (
4.27
) and
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
, we can expand
\mathcal{N}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{p}\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\left(\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{2}+2\overline{\mathcal{V}}\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right)+B_{p}\mathcal{U}^{p-3}\left(\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{3}+3\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{2}\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right)

(5.2)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-4}\left(\overline{\mathcal{V}}+\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right)^{4}+\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\left|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{p}\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\left(\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{2}+2\overline{\mathcal{V}}\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right)+B_{p}\mathcal{U}^{p-3}\left(\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{3}+3\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{2}\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-4\sigma}\left(\beta_{*}+Q^{\frac{2\wedge p}{2}}\left|\log Q\right|+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left|\left(\alpha_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-1}-1\right|\right)^{4}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\left(\chi_{p\geq 2}|\rho_{**}^{\perp}|^{2}+|\rho_{**}^{\perp}|^{p}\right)

in
\mathcal{A}_{*}
. In
\mathcal{A}_{*}^{c}
, if
\left|\mathcal{V}+\rho_{0}\right|\leq|\rho_{**}^{\perp}|
, then as that of (
4.4
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{O}\left(\chi_{p\geq 2}|\rho_{**}^{\perp}|^{2}+|\rho_{**}^{\perp}|^{p}\right).

(5.3)

Otherwise, we have
(\theta,t)\in\mathcal{C}\backslash\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{*}
and
|\rho_{**}^{\perp}|\leq\left|\mathcal{V}+\rho_{0}\right|
in
\mathcal{A}_{*}^{c}
. Since we always have
|\gamma_{ex}|=o(\mathcal{U})
by
(i)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
, as that of (
4.3
) and (
4.4
), either we have the expansion (
5.2
) if we further have
|\gamma_{*}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}|\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{U}
or we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{O}\left(|\gamma_{*}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}|^{2\wedge p}\right)

(5.4)

if we further have
\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{U}\leq|\gamma_{*}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}|
. Since by
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
, we have
\left|\overline{\mathcal{V}}\right|\lesssim\mathcal{U}^{1-\sigma}
and by (
4.27
), we can take
\sigma>0
arbitrary small if necessary. Thus, similar to (
4.5
),
we have

\displaystyle|\rho_{**}^{\perp}|^{1+\varepsilon}\gtrsim A_{p}\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\left(\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{2}+2\left|\overline{\mathcal{V}}\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right|\right)+B_{p}\mathcal{U}^{p-3}\left(\left|\overline{\mathcal{V}}\right|^{3}+3\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{2}|\rho_{**}^{\perp}|\right)

(5.5)

if
\left|\mathcal{V}+\rho_{0}\right|\leq|\rho_{**}^{\perp}|
in
\mathcal{A}^{c}
and

\displaystyle|\gamma_{*}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}|^{1+\varepsilon}\gtrsim A_{p}\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\left(\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{2}+2\left|\overline{\mathcal{V}}\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right|\right)+B_{p}\mathcal{U}^{p-3}\left(\left|\overline{\mathcal{V}}\right|^{3}+3\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{2}|\rho_{**}^{\perp}|\right)

(5.6)

if
(\theta,t)\in\mathcal{C}\backslash\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{*}
,
|\rho_{**}^{\perp}|\leq\left|\mathcal{V}+\rho_{0}\right|
and
\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{U}\leq|\gamma_{*}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}|
in
\mathcal{A}_{*}^{c}
, where
\varepsilon>0
is a constant which depends on
p>1
. The conclusion then follows from (
5.2
), (
5.3
), (
5.4
), (
5.5
) and (
5.6
).
∎

By multiplying (
3.16
) with
\Psi_{j}
on both sides and integrating by parts and by the orthogonal conditions of
\rho_{*}
given in (
3.16
) and the oddness of
\{\mathcal{V}_{i}\}
on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, we have

\displaystyle-\left\langle f,\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{1,j},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\left\langle\mathcal{N},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{1,i},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

(5.7)

\displaystyle+\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{1,ex},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\left\langle\mathcal{L}_{j,ex}(\rho_{*}),\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

for all
j=1,2,\cdots,\nu
. In what follows, by using the equality (
5.7
), we shall derive the estimate of
\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left|\left(\alpha_{j}^{*}\right)^{p}-\alpha_{j}^{*}\right|
.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
a<0
and
b=b_{FS}(a)
. Then we have

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left(\left(\alpha_{j}^{*}\right)^{p}-\alpha_{j}^{*}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left(\overline{B}_{1}+o(1)\right)Q-\left\langle f,\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\frac{\Psi_{j}}{\|\Psi\|^{2}+o(1)}\right\rangle

\displaystyle-\left(\overline{A}_{1}+o(1)\right)\beta_{*}^{2}+\mathcal{O}\left(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}\right),

where

\displaystyle\overline{B}_{1}=\frac{p\int_{\mathcal{C}}\Psi^{p-1}d\mu}{\|\Psi\|^{2}}\quad\text{and}\quad\overline{A}_{1}=\lim_{\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\to 0}\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}A_{p}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}}{\beta_{*}^{2}\|\Psi\|^{2}}.

Proof.

By the the orthogonal conditions of
\Psi_{j}
,
\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}
,
w_{j,l}
and
\rho_{*}
, and the oddness of
w_{j,l}
on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
and
\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}
in
\mathbb{R}
, we also have

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{j},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=A_{p}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+3B_{p}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\rho_{*},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

(5.8)

for all
j=1,2,\cdots,\nu
, where

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{j}=A_{p}\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\left(\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}+2\mathcal{V}_{j}\rho_{*}\right)+B_{p}\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\left(\mathcal{V}_{j}^{3}+3\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\rho_{*}\right).

(5.9)

Intersecting (
5.8
) into (
5.7
), we have

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle f,\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left(\left(\alpha_{j}^{*}\right)^{p}-\alpha_{j}^{*}\right)\|\Psi\|^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{1,i},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

(5.10)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{N}-\mathcal{N}_{j},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{j},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{1,ex},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{L}_{j,ex}(\rho_{*}),\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}.

The rest of the proof is to estimate every terms in (
5.10
).

Step. 1
The estimate of
\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{j},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By Lemma
3.1
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{j},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left(A_{p}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+3B_{p}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\rho_{*},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\overline{A}_{1,*}+o(1))\beta_{*}^{2},

where

\displaystyle\overline{A}_{1,*}=\lim_{\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\to 0}\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}A_{p}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}}{\beta_{*}^{2}}>0.

Step. 2
The estimate of
\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{1,i},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By (
3.18
) and Lemma
4.3
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{1,i},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left(\left(\alpha_{l}^{*}\right)^{p-1}-1\right)\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{p},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o(Q).

Step. 3
The estimate of
\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{1,ex},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By the Taylor expansion, (
3.18
) and Lemma
4.3
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{1,ex},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\left\langle p\Psi_{i}^{p-1}\mathcal{U}_{i}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\Psi_{j}^{p-1}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}},\mathcal{U}_{j}^{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\|\mathcal{U}\|_{L^{p+1}(\mathcal{C}\backslash\cup_{j=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{B}_{j})}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\overline{B}_{1,*}+o(1))Q,

where
\overline{B}_{1,*}=p\int_{\mathcal{C}}\Psi^{p-1}d\mu
is a positive constant.

Step. 4
The estimate of
\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{L}_{j,ex}(\rho_{*}),\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By (
3.17
) and
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{L}_{j,ex}(\rho_{*}),\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle p\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-1}-\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-1}\right)\rho_{*},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle p\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-1}-\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-1}\right)\Psi_{j},\rho_{0}+\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}.

Similar to (
4.30
),

\displaystyle\left|\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-1}-\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-1}\right)\Psi_{j}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\Psi_{i}^{p-1}\mathcal{U}_{i}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}}\right)+\mathcal{U}^{p}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\cup_{j=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{B}_{j}}.

By Lemma
4.3
and
(i)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-1}-\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-1}\right)\Psi_{j},\gamma_{ex}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}Q\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{p-\sigma}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{U}_{i}\right\rangle_{L^{2}},\quad p\geq 3,\\
&\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}Q\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{2p-3}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{U}_{i}\right\rangle_{L^{2}},\quad 1<p<3,\\
\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(Q).

By Lemma
4.3
and
(ii)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-1}-\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-1}\right)\Psi_{j},\gamma_{*}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\left(Q+\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}\left|\left(\alpha_{l}^{*}\right)^{p-1}-1\right|+\beta_{*}^{2}\right)\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{p-\sigma}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{U}_{i}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(Q).

By Lemma
4.3
and
(iii)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-1}-\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-1}\right)\Psi_{j},\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\beta_{*}^{2}\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{p-\sigma}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{U}_{i}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(Q).

By Lemma
4.3
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-1}-\Psi_{j}^{p-1}\right)\Psi_{j},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\langle\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\Psi_{i}^{p-1}\mathcal{U}_{i}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\left|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right|\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &Q\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|,\quad p>2,\\
&Q\left|\log Q\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|,\quad p=2,\\
&Q^{\frac{p}{2}}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|,\quad 1<p<2.\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(Q)+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2+\varepsilon}.

Summarizing the above estimates, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{L}_{j,ex}(\rho_{*}),\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o(Q)+\mathcal{O}\left(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2+\varepsilon}\right).

(5.11)

Step. 5
The estimate of
\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{N}-\mathcal{N}_{j},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By Lemmas
4.2
and
5.1
, (
5.9
) and the oddness of
\{\mathcal{V}_{i}\}
on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{N}-\mathcal{N}_{j},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{p}\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\left(\mathcal{V}_{i}^{2}+2\mathcal{V}_{i}\rho_{*}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

(5.12)

\displaystyle+3B_{p}\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{i}^{2}\rho_{*}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle-A_{p}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\left(\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}+2\mathcal{V}_{j}\rho_{*}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\mathcal{B}_{j}},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle-3B_{p}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\rho_{*}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\mathcal{B}_{j}},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}|\rho_{*}|\Psi_{i}^{\frac{3p-5}{2}}\mathcal{U}_{i}+\beta_{*}^{2}\Psi_{i}^{2p-2}\mathcal{U}_{i}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\left\langle\mathcal{O}\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\mathcal{V}^{2}+\beta_{*}|\rho_{*}|\mathcal{U}^{\frac{3(p-1)}{2}}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\left(\cup_{j=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{B}_{j}\right)},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\left\langle\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{rem}+\mathcal{N}_{0},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}.

Step. 5.1
The estimate of
\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\left(\mathcal{V}_{i}^{2}+2\mathcal{V}_{i}\rho_{*}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

By
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\left(\mathcal{V}_{i}^{2}+2\mathcal{V}_{i}\rho_{*}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\left(\mathcal{V}_{i}^{2}+2\mathcal{V}_{i}\gamma_{2,ex}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}+\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

(i)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\left(\beta_{*}^{2}\Psi_{i}^{2p-1}+\beta_{*}^{2}Q\Psi_{i}^{\frac{3p-1-2\sigma}{2}}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}},\quad p\geq\frac{7}{3},\\
&\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\left(\beta_{*}^{2}\Psi_{i}^{2p-1}+\beta_{*}^{2}Q\Psi_{i}^{3p-4}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}},\quad 1<p<\frac{7}{3}\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle o(Q).

By Lemma
4.3
and
(iii)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left|\left\langle\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{i}\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\beta_{*}^{3}\Psi_{i}^{\frac{3p-1-2\sigma}{2}}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}},\quad p\geq 3,\\
&\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\left(\beta_{*}^{3}\Psi_{i}^{\frac{3p-1-2\sigma}{2}}+\beta_{*}^{4}Q\Psi_{i}^{\frac{5p-7}{2}}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}},\quad 1<p<3\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(Q).

By Lemma
4.3
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left|\left\langle\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{i}\rho_{**}^{\perp}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\beta_{*}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|\left\|\Psi_{i}^{\frac{3p-3}{2}}\Psi_{j}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{B}_{i})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(Q)+\mathcal{O}\left(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2+\varepsilon}\right).

Summarizing the above estimates, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left|\left\langle\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\left(\mathcal{V}_{i}^{2}+2\mathcal{V}_{i}\rho_{*}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|=o(Q)+\mathcal{O}\left(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2+\varepsilon}\right).

(5.13)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(Q).

4.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\Psi_{j},\left|\gamma_{*}\right|^{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\lesssim\left(Q+\beta_{*}^{2}+\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}\left|\left(\alpha_{l}^{*}\right)^{p-1}-1\right|\right)^{2}.

By Lemma
4.3
and
(iii)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\Psi_{j},\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}^{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o(Q)+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}^{2}+\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}\left|\left(\alpha_{l}^{*}\right)^{p-1}-1\right|\right)^{2}.

Thus, summarizing the above estimates, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{0},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|\lesssim\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2}+o(Q)+\left(\beta_{*}^{2}+\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}\left|\left(\alpha_{l}^{*}\right)^{p-1}-1\right|\right)^{2}.

Step. 5.9
The estimate of
\left\langle\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{rem},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By (
4.27
), Lemma
5.1
and
(ii)
and
(iii)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{rem},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|\lesssim\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\left(\beta_{*}+\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}\left|\left(\alpha_{l}^{*}\right)^{p-1}-1\right|\right)^{4}+o(Q).

By summarizing the estimates from Step. 5.1 to Step. 5.9, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mathcal{N}-\mathcal{N}_{j},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|\lesssim\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+o\left(Q\right)+\left(\beta_{*}^{2}+\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}\left|\left(\alpha_{l}^{*}\right)^{p-1}-1\right|\right)^{2}.

The conclusion follows from the estimates from Step. 1 to Step.5.
∎

6.
Final expansion of
\mathcal{N}
and estimates of
Q

Again, we emphasize that we need to eliminate the lower order terms (compared to the
\beta_{*}^{4}
terms) in the data
\mathcal{R}_{new,0}
which is given in
(2)
of Proposition
4.1
to get the desired stability inequality.
Thus, we need to finally further expand
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{rem}
, the remaining term in the expansion of
\mathcal{N}
given by Lemma
5.1
, into higher order terms.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
a<0
and
b=b_{FS}(a)
. Then
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{rem}
, the remaining term in the expansion of
\mathcal{N}
given by Lemma
5.1
, can be further expanded as follows:

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{rem}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{p}\mathcal{U}^{p-4}\left(\overline{\mathcal{V}}+\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right)^{4}+D_{p}\mathcal{U}^{p-5}\left(\overline{\mathcal{V}}+\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right)^{5}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-6\sigma}\left(\beta_{*}+Q^{\frac{2\wedge p}{2}}+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left|\left(\alpha_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-1}-1\right|\right)^{6}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\left(\chi_{p\geq 2}|\rho_{**}^{\perp}|^{2}+|\rho_{**}^{\perp}|^{p}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{p}\mathcal{U}^{p-4}\left(\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{4}+4\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{3}\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right)+D_{p}\mathcal{U}^{p-5}\left(\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{5}+5\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{4}\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-6\sigma}\left(\beta_{*}+Q^{\frac{2\wedge p}{2}}\left|\log Q\right|+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left|\left(\alpha_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-1}-1\right|\right)^{6}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\left(\chi_{p\geq 2}|\rho_{**}^{\perp}|^{2}+|\rho_{**}^{\perp}|^{p}+|\gamma_{*}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}|^{1+\varepsilon}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{*}}\right)

in
\mathcal{C}
where
\overline{\mathcal{V}}=\mathcal{V}+\rho_{0}
with
\mathcal{V}
given by (
3.15
),
\rho_{0}
given in Proposition
4.1
,
C_{p}=\frac{p(p-1)(p-2)(p-3)}{24}
,
D_{p}=\frac{p(p-1)(p-2)(p-3)(p-4)}{120}
and and
\sigma
and
\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{*}
are given by (
4.27
).

Proof.

The proof is a direct application of the Taylor expansion to
\mathcal{N}
in the set
\mathcal{A}_{*}
, which is introduced in the proof of Lemma
5.1
, up to the sixth order term.
∎

By multiplying (
3.16
) with
\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}
on both sides and integrating by parts, the orthogonal conditions of
\rho_{*}
given in (
3.16
) and the oddness of
\{\mathcal{V}_{i}\}
on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
and
\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}
in
\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle-\left\langle f,\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{1,i},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{L}_{j,ex}(\rho_{*}),\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

(6.1)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{N},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{1,ex},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}.

In what follows, we shall derive the estimate of
Q
from (
6.1
).

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
a<0
and
b=b_{FS}(a)
. Then we have

\displaystyle Q=\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}^{6}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Proof.

By the the orthogonal conditions of
\Psi_{j}
,
\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}
,
w_{j,l}
and
\rho_{*}
, and the oddness of
w_{j,l}
on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
and
\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}
in
\mathbb{R}
, we also have

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{j},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=2A_{p}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}\rho_{*},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+3B_{p}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\rho_{*},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

(6.2)

for all
j=1,2,\cdots,\nu
, where
\mathcal{N}_{j}
is given by (
5.9
).
Intersecting (
6.2
) into (
6.1
), we have

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle f,\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{1,ex},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{j},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

(6.3)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{N}-\mathcal{N}_{j},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{L}_{j,ex}(\rho_{*}),\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{1,i},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}.

As in the proof of Proposition
4.1
, the rest of the proof is to estimate every terms in (
6.3
).

Step. 1
The estimate of
\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{1,ex},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By (
2.7
), (
3.18
), Lemma
4.3
and the Taylor expansion,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{1,ex},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{i}}\left(\mathcal{U}^{p}-\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}\left(\Psi_{l}^{*}\right)^{p}\right)\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}d\mu+\mathcal{O}\left(Q^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}p\int_{\mathcal{B}_{j}}\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-1}\left(\Psi_{j+1}^{*}+\Psi_{j-1}^{*}\right)\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}d\mu

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{i}}\Psi_{i}^{p-1}\mathcal{U}_{i}^{2}d\mu\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(Q^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p\int_{\mathcal{B}_{j}}\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-1}\left(\Psi_{j+1}^{*}+\Psi_{j-1}^{*}\right)\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}d\mu+o(Q),

where by (
2.7
) and Lemma
4.3
again,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}p\int_{\mathcal{B}_{j}}\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-1}\left(\Psi_{j+1}^{*}+\Psi_{j-1}^{*}\right)\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}d\mu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\frac{\int_{\mathcal{C}}\partial_{t}\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p}\left(\Psi_{j+1}^{*}+\Psi_{j-1}^{*}\right)d\mu}{\alpha_{j}^{*}}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\left(Q^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\frac{\int_{\mathcal{C}}\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p}\partial_{t}\left(\Psi_{j+1}^{*}+\Psi_{j-1}^{*}\right)d\mu}{\alpha_{j}^{*}}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\left(Q^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(B_{2}+o(1))Q

with
B_{2}>0
being a constant.
Thus, summarizing the above estimates, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{1,ex},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=(B_{2}+o(1))Q.

Step. 2
The estimate of
\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{1,i},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By (
3.18
) and Lemma
4.3
,

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{1,i},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|\lesssim\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left|\left(\alpha_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-1}-1\right|\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{p},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o(Q).

Step. 3
The estimate of
\left\langle\mathcal{L}_{j,ex}(\rho_{*}),\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By (
2.7
) and (
5.11
),

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mathcal{L}_{j,ex}(\rho_{*}),\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|\lesssim\left\langle\left|\mathcal{L}_{j,ex}(\rho_{*})\right|,\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o\left(Q\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2+\varepsilon}\right).

Step. 4
The estimate of
\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{j},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By (
6.2
) and
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{j},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2A_{p}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}\partial_{t}\Psi_{j},\gamma_{2,ex}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,rem,j}+\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+3B_{p}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\partial_{t}\Psi_{j},\gamma_{1,ex}+\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+3B_{p}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\partial_{t}\Psi_{j},\sum_{l=1;l\not=j}^{\nu}\gamma_{1,l}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,rem,j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}.

By (
2.7
) and Lemma
4.3
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\partial_{t}\Psi_{j},\gamma_{1,ex}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\beta_{*}^{2}Q\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{1-\sigma}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\Psi_{j}^{2p-1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}},\quad p\geq 3,\\
&\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\beta_{*}^{2}Q\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{p-2}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\Psi_{j}^{2p-1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}},\quad 1<p<3\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(Q).

By (
2.7
), Lemma
4.5
and
(iii)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\partial_{t}\Psi_{j},\sum_{l=1;l\not=j}^{\nu}\gamma_{1,l}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,rem,j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\beta_{*}^{2}\left(\beta_{*}^{2}+\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left|\left(\alpha_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-1}-1\right|\right)\sum_{l=1;l\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{1-\sigma}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{l}},\Psi_{j}^{2p-1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(Q).

By (
2.7
), Lemma
4.3
and
(i)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}\partial_{t}\Psi_{j},\gamma_{2,ex}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\beta_{*}^{2}Q\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{1-\sigma}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\Psi_{j}^{\frac{3p-1}{2}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}},\quad p\geq\frac{7}{3},\\
&\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\beta_{*}^{2}Q\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{\frac{3p-5}{2}}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\Psi_{j}^{\frac{3p-1}{2}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}},\quad 1<p<\frac{7}{3}\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(Q).

By (
2.7
), Lemma
4.3
and
(iii)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}\partial_{t}\Psi_{j},\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,rem,j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|\lesssim\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\beta_{*}^{3}\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{1-\sigma}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\Psi_{j}^{\frac{3p-1}{2}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o(Q).

Thus, summarizing the above estimates, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{j},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o(Q)+\mathcal{O}\left(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2+\varepsilon}\right).

Step. 5
The estimate of
\left\langle\mathcal{N}-\mathcal{N}_{j},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

Since
\left|\partial_{t}\Psi\right|\lesssim\Psi
by (
2.7
), we can use similar estimates of (
5.12
) to obtain

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mathcal{N}-\mathcal{N}_{j},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}-\left\langle\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{rem}+\mathcal{N}_{0},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|=o(Q)+\mathcal{O}\left(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2+\varepsilon}\right).

Step. 5.1
The estimate of
\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{0},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

Step. 5.1.1
The estimate of
\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{0}-\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{0,1},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
, where

\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{0,1}=A_{p}\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\rho_{0}^{2}+B_{p}\mathcal{U}^{p-3}\left(3\mathcal{V}\rho_{0}^{2}+\rho_{0}^{3}\right)
.

By (
4.2
),
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
and Lemma
5.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{0}-\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{0,1},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\lesssim\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-2-\sigma}\Psi_{j}\left(\left|\gamma_{ex}\right|+\left|\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}+\gamma_{*}\right|\right),\left|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right|\right\rangle_{L^{2}}.

By Lemma
4.3
and
(i)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-2-\sigma}\Psi_{j}\left|\gamma_{ex}\right|,\left|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right|\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}Q\|\Psi_{i}^{p-1-2\sigma}\Psi_{j}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{B}_{i})}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|,\quad p\geq 3,\\
&\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}Q\|\Psi_{i}^{2(p-2)-\sigma}\Psi_{j}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{B}_{i})}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|,\quad 1<p<3\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(Q)+\mathcal{O}\left(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2+\varepsilon}\right).

By Lemma
4.3
,
(ii)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
and Proposition
5.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\Psi_{j}\left|\gamma_{*}\right|,\left|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right|\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(Q+\beta_{*}^{2}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right)\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(Q+\beta_{*}^{6})+\mathcal{O}\left(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

By Lemma
4.3
and
(iii)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\Psi_{j}\left|\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}\right|,\left|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right|\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\beta_{*}^{2}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|,\quad p\geq 3,\\
&\left(\beta_{*}^{2}+\beta_{*}^{3}Q\right)\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|,\quad 1<p<3\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(\beta_{*}^{6})+\mathcal{O}\left(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}\right).

Summarizing the above estimates, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{0}-\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{0,1},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o(Q+\beta_{*}^{6})+\mathcal{O}\left(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Step. 5.1.2
The estimate of
\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\rho_{0}^{2},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\rho_{0}^{2},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\left(\gamma_{*}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}\right)^{2},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\gamma_{ex}^{2},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+2\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\left(\gamma_{*}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}\right)\gamma_{ex},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}.

By (
2.7
) and similar estimates of (
5.13
),

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\gamma_{ex}^{2},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|=o(Q).

By (
2.7
) and
(i)
and
(ii)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\gamma_{*}\gamma_{ex},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}o\left(Q\left\|\Psi_{i}^{p-2\sigma}\Psi_{j}\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{B}_{i})}\right),\quad p\geq 3,\\
&\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}o\left(Q\left\|\Psi_{i}^{2p-3-\sigma}\Psi_{j}\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{B}_{i})}\right),\quad 1<p<3\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(Q).

By (
2.7
) and
(i)
and
(iii)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}\gamma_{ex},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o(Q).

By the oddness of
\partial_{t}\Psi
in
\mathbb{R}
and
(ii)
and
(iii)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\left(\gamma_{*}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}\right)^{2},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-2}-\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\right)\partial_{t}\Psi_{j},\mathcal{W}_{sym,j}^{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\mathcal{W}_{*,j},2\mathcal{W}_{sym,j}+\mathcal{W}_{*,j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}},

where
\mathcal{W}_{sym,j}=\gamma_{1,j}+\rho_{**,1,j}^{\perp}-\alpha_{j,1}^{**}\Psi_{j}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j}

and
\mathcal{W}_{*,j}=\gamma_{*}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}-\mathcal{W}_{sym,j}
.
Similar to (
4.30
), by (
2.7
),

\displaystyle\left|\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-2}-\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\right)\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right|\lesssim\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\Psi_{i}^{p-2}\mathcal{U}_{i}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}}\right)+\mathcal{U}^{p-1}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\cup_{j=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{B}_{j}},

thus, by Lemmas
4.3
,
4.5
,
4.8
,
4.9
and Proposition
5.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-2}-\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\right)\partial_{t}\Psi_{j},\mathcal{W}_{sym,j}^{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\Psi_{j}^{p-2\sigma}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}},\mathcal{U}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right)=o(Q).

Since by (
2.7
), Lemma
4.5
,
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
and Proposition
5.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\mathcal{W}_{*,j}\left(2\mathcal{W}_{sym,j}+\mathcal{W}_{*,j}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(\beta_{*}^{2}+Q+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right)^{2}Q^{1-\sigma}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\Psi_{j}^{1-\sigma}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}}+\mathcal{U}^{1-\sigma}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\cup_{j=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{B}_{j}}\right)

in
\mathcal{C}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\mathcal{W}_{*,j},2\mathcal{W}_{sym,j}+\mathcal{W}_{*,j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o(Q+\beta_{*}^{6})+\mathcal{O}\left(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Summarizing the above estimates, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\rho_{0}^{2},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o(Q+\beta_{*}^{6})+\mathcal{O}\left(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Step. 5.1.3
The estimate of
\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-3}\mathcal{V}\rho_{0}^{2},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

Clearly, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-3}\mathcal{V}\rho_{0}^{2},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-3}\left(\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\mathcal{V}_{i}\right)\rho_{0}^{2},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{j}\rho_{0}^{2},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}.

By (
4.2
) and applying the same symmetry as in the estimate of
\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\rho_{0}^{2},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{j}\rho_{0}^{2},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o(Q+\beta_{*}^{6})+\mathcal{O}\left(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

By (
2.7
) and
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-3}\left(\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\mathcal{V}_{i}\right)\rho_{0}^{2},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-3}\left|\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\mathcal{V}_{i}\right|\gamma_{ex}^{2},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-3}\left|\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\mathcal{V}_{i}\right|\left(\gamma_{*}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}\right)^{2},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}.

By Lemma
4.3
and
(i)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-3}\left|\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\mathcal{V}_{i}\right|\gamma_{ex}^{2},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\beta_{*}Q^{2}\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{\frac{3p-1-4\sigma}{2}}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{U}_{i}\right\rangle_{L^{2}},\quad p\geq 3,\\
&\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\beta_{*}Q^{2}\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{\frac{7p-13}{2}}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{U}_{i}\right\rangle_{L^{2}},\quad 1<p<3\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(Q).

By Lemma
4.3
,
(ii)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
and Proposition
5.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-3}\left|\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\mathcal{V}_{i}\right|\gamma_{*}^{2},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\beta_{*}\left(Q+\beta_{*}^{2}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right)^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{\frac{3p-1-4\sigma}{2}}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{U}_{i}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(Q).

By Lemma
4.3
and
(iii)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-3}\left|\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\mathcal{V}_{i}\right|\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}^{2},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o(Q).

Summarizing the above estimates, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-3}\mathcal{V}\rho_{0}^{2},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o(Q+\beta_{*}^{6})+\mathcal{O}\left(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Step. 5.1.4
The estimate of
\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-3}\rho_{0}^{3},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
and Proposition
5.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-3}\rho_{0}^{3},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(Q^{(2p-1)\wedge 3}\left|\log Q\right|+\left(\beta_{*}^{2}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right)^{3}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(Q+\beta_{*}^{6})+\mathcal{O}\left(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Summarizing the estimates from Step. 5.1.1 to Step. 5.1.4, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{0},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o(Q+\beta_{*}^{6})+\mathcal{O}\left(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Step. 5.2
The estimate of
\left\langle\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{rem},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By Lemma
6.1
and Proposition
5.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{rem},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{p}\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-4}\left(\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{4}+4\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{3}\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right),\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+D_{p}\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-5}\left(\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{5}+5\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{4}\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right),\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+o(Q)+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}^{6}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Step. 5.2.1
The estimates of
\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-4}\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{3}\rho_{**}^{\perp},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
and
\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-5}\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{4}\rho_{**}^{\perp},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

Recall that
\overline{\mathcal{V}}=\mathcal{V}+\rho_{0}
with
\mathcal{V}
given by (
3.15
) and
\rho_{0}
given in Proposition
4.1
. By (
2.7
),
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
and Proposition
5.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-4}\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{3}\rho_{**}^{\perp},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(Q^{\frac{4p-3}{2}\wedge 3}\left|\log Q\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\beta_{*}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right)^{3}\right)\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(Q+\beta_{*}^{6})+\mathcal{O}\left(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

and

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-5}\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{4}\rho_{**}^{\perp},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(Q^{\frac{5p-4}{2}\wedge 3}\left|\log Q\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\beta_{*}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right)^{4}\right)\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(Q+\beta_{*}^{6})+\mathcal{O}\left(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Step. 5.2.2
The estimates of
\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-4}\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{4},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
and
\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-5}\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{5},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By the oddness of
\partial_{t}\Psi
in
\mathbb{R}
, Lemmas
4.5
,
4.8
and
4.9
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-4}\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{4},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-4}-\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-4}\right)\partial_{t}\Psi_{j},\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{sym,j}^{4}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-4}\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{*,j},4\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{sym,j}^{3}+6\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{sym,j}^{2}\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{*,j}+4\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{sym,j}\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{*,j}^{2}+\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{*,j}^{3}\right\rangle_{L^{2}},

where
\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{sym,j}=\mathcal{V}_{j}+\gamma_{1,j}+\rho_{**,1,j}^{\perp}-\alpha_{j,1}^{**}\Psi_{j}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j}

and
\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{*,j}=\overline{\mathcal{V}}-\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{sym,j}
.
Similar to (
4.30
), by (
2.7
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-4}-\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-4}\right)\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right|\lesssim\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\Psi_{i}^{p-4}\mathcal{U}_{i}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}}\right)+\mathcal{U}^{p-1}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\cup_{j=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{B}_{j}},

thus, by (
3.15
), Lemmas
4.3
,
4.5
,
4.8
,
4.9
and Proposition
5.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-4}-\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-4}\right)\partial_{t}\Psi_{j},\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{sym,j}^{4}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o\left(\left\langle\Psi_{j}^{p-4\sigma}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}},\mathcal{U}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right)=o(Q).

Since by (
2.7
), (
3.15
), Lemma
4.5
,
(iii)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
and Proposition
5.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{*,j}\left(4\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{sym,j}^{3}+6\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{sym,j}^{2}\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{*,j}+4\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{sym,j}\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{*,j}^{2}+\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{*,j}^{3}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle Q^{1-\sigma}\left(Q+\beta_{*}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right)^{3}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\Psi_{j}^{3-3\sigma}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}}+\mathcal{U}^{3-3\sigma}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\cup_{j=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{B}_{j}}\right)

in
\mathcal{C}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-4}\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{*,j},4\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{sym,j}^{3}+6\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{sym,j}^{2}\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{*,j}+4\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{sym,j}\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{*,j}^{2}+\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{*,j}^{3}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle Q^{1-\sigma}\left(Q+\beta_{*}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right)^{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(Q+\beta_{*}^{6})+\mathcal{O}\left(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Summarizing the above estimates, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-4}\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{4},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o(Q)+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}^{6}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

By (
4.2
) and applying the same symmetry as in the estimate of
\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-4}\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{4},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
, we also have

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-5}\overline{\mathcal{V}}^{5},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o(Q+\beta_{*}^{6})+\mathcal{O}\left(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Summarizing the estimates from Step. 5.2.1 to Step. 5.2.2, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{rem},\partial_{t}\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o(Q)+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}^{6}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

The conclusion follows from the estimates from Step. 1 to Step. 5.
∎

7.
Estimate of
\rho_{**}^{\perp}

By the orthogonal conditions of
\rho_{**}^{\perp}
, given by (
4.43
) and multiplying (
4.52
) which is given in
(2)
of Propostion
4.1
with
\rho_{**}^{\perp}
on both sides and integrating by parts, we have

\displaystyle\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2}\lesssim\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|+\left|\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{new,0},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|,

(7.1)

where
\mathcal{R}_{new,0}
is given by (
4.53
) which is given in
(2)
of Propostion
4.1
. Moreover, we remark that by Lemmas
4.1
and
5.1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{rem}=\mathcal{N}_{0}+\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{rem},

(7.2)

where
\mathcal{N}_{rem}
is the remaining term in
\mathcal{R}_{new,0}
. We emphasize once more that we need to eliminate the lower order terms (compared to the
\beta_{*}^{4}
terms) in the data
\mathcal{R}_{new,0}
which is given in
(2)
of Proposition
4.1
to get the desired stability inequality. Thus, we
need further decompose
\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\gamma_{1,j}
which is given in Lemma
4.5
.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
a<0
and
b=b_{FS}(a)
. Then we have the following decomposition

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\gamma_{1,j}=\gamma_{1,*}+\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}\alpha_{l}^{***}\Psi_{l},

where
\{\alpha_{l}^{***}\}
is chosen such that
\left\langle\gamma_{1,*},\Psi_{l}\right\rangle=0
for all
1\leq l\leq\nu
. Moreover, we have the following estimates

\displaystyle\|\gamma_{1,*}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}Q^{1-\sigma}\left|\left(\alpha_{l}^{*}\right)^{p}-\alpha_{l}^{*}\right|\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left|\alpha_{j}^{***}\right|\lesssim\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}\left|\left(\alpha_{l}^{*}\right)^{p}-\alpha_{l}^{*}\right|.

Proof.

By the orthogonal conditions of
\gamma_{1,*}
and multiplying (
4.25
) with
\Psi_{j}
on both sides and integrating by parts, we have

\displaystyle\|\Psi\|^{2}\alpha_{j}^{***}+\sum_{l=1;l\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\Psi_{j},\Psi_{l}\right\rangle\alpha_{l}^{***}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{1,j},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\sum_{l=1;l\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{1,l},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

(7.3)

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}p\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-1}\gamma_{1,l},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{1,j},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+p\sum_{l=1;l\not=j}\alpha_{l}^{***}\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-1},\Psi_{j}\Psi_{l}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+p\alpha_{j}^{***}\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-1},\Psi_{j}^{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\sum_{l=1;l\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{1,l},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+p\left\langle\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-1}-\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-1}\right)\Psi_{j},\gamma_{1,*}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

for all
1\leq j\leq\nu
and
\gamma_{1,*}
satisfies the following equation:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{L}(\gamma_{1,*})=\mathcal{R}_{1,*}-\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\Psi_{i}^{p-1}\left(c_{1,j,i}\partial_{t}\Psi_{i}+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\varsigma_{1,j,i,l}w_{i,l}\right),\quad\text{in }\mathcal{C},\\
&\langle\partial_{t}\Psi_{j},\gamma_{1,*}\rangle=\langle w_{j,l},\gamma_{1,*}\rangle=0\quad\text{for all }1\leq j\leq\nu\text{ and }1\leq l\leq d,\end{aligned}\right.

(7.4)

where by (
3.18
),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1,*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}\left(\mathcal{R}_{1,l}-\alpha_{l}^{***}\left(\Psi_{l}^{p}-p\mathcal{U}^{p-1}\Psi_{l}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}\left(\left(\alpha_{l}^{*}\right)^{p}-\alpha_{l}^{*}-\alpha_{l}^{***}\left(1-p\left(\alpha_{l}^{*}\right)^{p-1}\right)\right)\Psi_{l}^{p}

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}p\alpha_{l}^{***}\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-1}-\left(\Psi_{l}^{*}\right)^{p-1}\right)\Psi_{l}.

By Lemma
4.3
, (
3.18
) and (
7.3
), we have

\displaystyle\left(1-p\left(\alpha_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-1}\right)\alpha_{j}^{***}=\left(\left(\alpha_{j}^{*}\right)^{p}-\alpha_{j}^{*}\right)+\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{O}(Q)\left(\left(\alpha_{l}^{*}\right)^{p}-\alpha_{l}^{*}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(Q^{1-\sigma}\right)\|\gamma_{1,*}\|

for all
1\leq j\leq\nu
. Thus, by Lemma
4.5
, (
7.4
), the orthogonal conditions of
\gamma_{1,j}
given in (
4.25
) and the elliptic estimates, we have the desired estimates of
\|\gamma_{1,*}\|_{L^{\infty}}
and
\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left|\alpha_{j}^{***}\right|
.
∎

In what follows, we shall estimate
\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|
by (
7.1
).

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
a<0
and
b=b_{FS}(a)
. Then we have

\displaystyle\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|\lesssim\beta_{*}^{4}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}.

Proof.

By (
4.53
) and (
7.2
),

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{new,0},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\left\langle(c_{1,ex,i}+c_{1,j,i}+c_{3,led,i}-c_{new,*,i})\Psi_{i}^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi_{i},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\sum_{l=1}^{d}\left\langle(\varsigma_{2,ex,i,l}+\varsigma_{3,led,i,l}-\varsigma_{new,*,i,l})\Psi_{i}^{p-1}w_{i,l},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}2A_{p}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}(\rho_{*}-\gamma_{1,ex}-\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j})\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}3B_{p}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\rho_{*}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{2,j},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\mathcal{U}_{j}\left(\beta_{*}\Psi_{j}^{2p-3}\left(\Psi_{j}+\rho_{*}\right)+|\rho_{*}-\gamma_{1,ex}|\Psi_{j}^{\frac{3p-5}{2}}\right)\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\alpha_{j}^{**}\left\langle\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-1}-\Psi_{j}^{p-1}\right)\Psi_{j},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\left\langle\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{rem},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{0},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\left\langle\mathcal{O}\left(|\rho_{*}-\gamma_{1,ex}|\mathcal{U}^{\frac{3p-3}{2}}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\cup_{j=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{B}_{j}},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}.

By the orthogonal conditions of
\rho_{**}^{\perp}
given by (
4.43
) once more,

\displaystyle\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{p-1}(c_{1,ex,i}+c_{1,j,i}+c_{3,led,i}-c_{new,*,i})\partial_{t}\Psi_{i},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=0

and

\displaystyle\left\langle\sum_{l=1}^{d}(\varsigma_{2,ex,j,l}+\varsigma_{3,led,j,l}-\varsigma_{new,*,i,l})w_{i,l},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=0

for all
1\leq i,j\leq\nu
, and further by (
3.19
), we also have

\displaystyle\left\langle\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{R}_{2,i},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=0.

Step. 1
The estimate of
\left\langle\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{rem},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By Lemma
5.1
and Propositions
5.1
and
6.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{rem},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|\lesssim\beta_{*}^{4}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2+\varepsilon}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|\|f\|_{H^{-1}}.

Step. 2
The estimate of
\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{0},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By Lemma
5.1
,
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
and Propositions
5.1
and
6.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{0},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\rho_{0}^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|+o(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(\left\|\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\gamma_{ex}^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}+\beta_{*}^{4}\right)\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|+o(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2}).

By Lemma
4.3
,
(i)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
and Proposition
6.1
,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\gamma_{1,ex}^{2}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &Q^{4}\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\|\Psi_{j}\right\|_{L^{2p-4\sigma}(\mathcal{B}_{j})}^{2p-4\sigma},\quad p\geq 3,\\
&Q^{4}\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\|\Psi_{j}\right\|_{L^{6(p-2)}(\mathcal{B}_{j})}^{6(p-2)},\quad 1<p<3,\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(\beta_{*}^{6}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}).

Summarizing the above estimates, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{0},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\|f\|_{H^{-1}}+\beta_{*}^{4}\right)\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|\right)+o(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2}).

Step. 3
The estimate of
\left\langle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\alpha_{j}^{**}\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-1}-\Psi_{j}^{p-1}\right)\Psi_{j},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By the orthogonal conditions of
\rho_{**}^{\perp}
given by (
4.43
), we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\alpha_{j}^{**}\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-1}-\Psi_{j}^{p-1}\right)\Psi_{j},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=\left\langle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\alpha_{j}^{**}\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-1}-\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-1}\right)\Psi_{j},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}.

Similar to (
4.30
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\alpha_{j}^{**}\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-1}-\Psi_{j}^{p-1}\right)\Psi_{j}\right|\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left|\alpha_{j}^{**}\right|\Psi_{j}^{p-1}\mathcal{U}_{j}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}}+\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left|\alpha_{j}^{**}\right|\right)\mathcal{U}^{p}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\cup_{j=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{B}_{j}}.

Thus, by Lemma
4.3

(2)
of Proposition
4.1
and Propositions
5.1
and
6.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\alpha_{j}^{**}\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-1}-\Psi_{j}^{p-1}\right)\Psi_{j},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left|\alpha_{j}^{**}\right|Q^{\frac{1}{2}+\sigma}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right)\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|\right)+o(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2}).

Step. 4
The estimate of
\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\beta_{*}^{2}\Psi_{j}^{2(p-1)}\mathcal{U}_{j}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By Lemma
4.3
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\beta_{*}^{2}\Psi_{j}^{2(p-1)}\mathcal{U}_{j}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=
