\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}^{2}Q^{\frac{1}{2}+\sigma}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right)\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|\right)+o(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2}).

Step. 5
The estimates of

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\beta_{*}^{2}\mathcal{U}_{j}\left(\Psi_{j}^{2p-3}\left(\Psi_{j}+\gamma_{1,ex}\right)\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

and
\left\langle\beta_{*}(\rho_{*}-\gamma_{1,ex})\mathcal{U}_{*},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
, where
\mathcal{U}_{*}=\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\Psi_{j}^{\frac{3p-5}{2}}\mathcal{U}_{j}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}}+\mathcal{U}^{\frac{3(p-1)}{2}}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\left(\cup_{j=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{B}_{j}\right)}
.

By Lemma
4.3
and Proposition
6.1

\displaystyle\left\langle\beta_{*}^{2}\mathcal{U}_{j}\Psi_{j}^{2p-2}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o\left(\beta_{*}^{4}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2+\varepsilon}\right).

By Lemma
4.3
,
(i)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
and Proposition
6.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\beta_{*}^{2}\mathcal{U}_{j}\Psi_{j}^{2p-3}\gamma_{1,ex}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\left\langle\beta_{*}^{2}\mathcal{U}_{j}\Psi_{j}^{2p-2-\sigma}\left(Q_{j}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j,+}}+Q_{j-1}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j,-}}\right),\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}},\quad p\geq 3,\\
&\left\langle\beta_{*}^{2}\mathcal{U}_{j}\Psi_{j}^{3p-5}\left(Q_{j}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j,+}}+Q_{j-1}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j,-}}\right),\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}},\quad 1<p<3\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o\left(\beta_{*}^{4}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2+\varepsilon}\right).

By
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\beta_{*}(\rho_{*}-\gamma_{1,ex})\mathcal{U}_{*},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=\beta_{*}\left\langle\gamma_{2,ex}+\gamma_{*}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led},\mathcal{U}_{*}\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\beta_{*}\left\langle\mathcal{U}_{*},\left(\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right)^{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}.

Since
\|\mathcal{U}_{*}\|_{L^{\infty}}=o(1)
, we have
\beta_{*}\left\langle\mathcal{U}_{*},\left|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right|^{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o\left(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2}\right)
.
By Lemma
4.3
,
(i)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
and Proposition
6.1
, we have

\displaystyle\beta_{*}\left|\left\langle\gamma_{2,ex},\mathcal{U}_{*}\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|\

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\beta_{*}^{2}Q\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{3p-3-2\sigma},\mathcal{U}_{i}^{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{B}_{i})}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},\quad p\geq\frac{7}{3},\\
&\beta_{*}^{2}Q\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{6p-10},\mathcal{U}_{i}^{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{B}_{i})}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},\quad 1<p<\frac{7}{3}\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o\left(\beta_{*}^{4}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2+\varepsilon}\right).

By Lemma
4.3
,
(ii)
and
(iii)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
and Propositions
5.1
and
6.1
, we have

\displaystyle\beta_{*}\left\langle\gamma_{*}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led},\mathcal{U}_{*}\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\beta_{*}^{3}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left(\left\|\Psi_{j}^{\frac{3(p-1)-2\sigma}{2}}\mathcal{U}_{j}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{B}_{j})}\right)+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|

\displaystyle+\beta_{*}^{4}Q^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o\left(\beta_{*}^{4}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2+\varepsilon}\right).

Summarizing the above estimates, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\beta_{*}\rho_{*}\mathcal{U}_{*},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o\left(\beta_{*}^{4}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2+\varepsilon}\right).

Step. 6
The estimates of

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}(\rho_{*}-\gamma_{1,ex}-\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j})\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

and

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\rho_{*}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}.

By
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\left(2A_{p}\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}(\rho_{*}-\gamma_{1,ex}-\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j})+3B_{p}\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\rho_{*}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{V}_{*}\rho_{0},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}-\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle 2A_{p}\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}(\gamma_{1,ex}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j})\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+o\left(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2}\right).

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{*}=\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left(2A_{p}\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}+3B_{p}\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}}.

By Lemma
4.3
,
(i)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
and Propositions
5.1
and
6.1
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\gamma_{2,ex},\mathcal{V}_{*}\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\beta_{*}^{2}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}Q_{j}\left\|\Psi_{j}^{\frac{3p-1-2\sigma}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{B}_{j})}\right),\quad p\geq\frac{7}{3},\\
&\beta_{*}^{2}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\nu-1}Q_{j}\left\|\Psi_{j}^{3p-4}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{B}_{j})}\right),\quad 1<p<\frac{7}{3}\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o\left(\beta_{*}^{4}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2+\varepsilon}\right).

By the orthogonal conditions of
\rho_{**}^{\perp}
given in
(2)
of Proposition
4.1
, Lemmas
4.5
and
4.10
,
(iii)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
and Propositions
5.1
and
6.1
and Lemma
7.1
that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}(\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}\gamma_{1,l}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}-\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j})\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\left(\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}\gamma_{1,l}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left|\left\langle\gamma_{1,*}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,rem,j,*}+\rho_{**,2}^{\perp}+\sum_{l=1;l\not=j}^{\nu}\left(\alpha_{l}^{***}-\alpha_{l,1}^{**}\right)\Psi_{l},\Psi_{j}^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{B}_{j})}\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\left\langle\left(\alpha_{j}^{***}-\alpha_{j,1}^{**}\right)\Psi_{j},\Psi_{j}^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{C}\backslash\mathcal{B}_{j})}\right|+\beta_{*}^{2}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|\left(\beta_{*}^{2}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\beta_{*}^{4}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|\|f\|_{H^{-1}}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2+\varepsilon}.

Summarizing the above estimates, we have

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\left(2A_{p}\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}(\rho_{*}-\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led,j})+3B_{p}\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\rho_{*}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\beta_{*}^{4}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2+\varepsilon}.

The conclusion follows from the estimates from Step. 1 to Step. 6.
∎

8.
Estimate of
\beta_{*}
and proof of
(a)
of Theorem
1.3

By multiplying (
3.16
) with
\mathcal{V}_{j}
on both sides and integrating by parts, the orthogonal conditions of
\rho_{*}
and the oddness of
\{\mathcal{V}_{i}\}
on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, we have

\displaystyle-\left\langle f,\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{2,j},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\left\langle\mathcal{N},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\left\langle\mathcal{L}_{j,ex}(\rho_{*}),\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

(8.1)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{2,i},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{2,ex},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

for all
j=1,2,\cdots,\nu
.

Proposition 8.1
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
a<0
and
b=b_{FS}(a)
. Then we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left(p\left((\alpha_{j}^{*})^{p-1}-1\right)\left\|\Psi_{j}^{p-1}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\right\|_{L^{1}}+\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{j},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\left\langle f,\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{H^{1}}\right)=o(\beta_{*}^{4})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right),

where
\mathcal{N}_{j}
is given by (
5.9
).

Proof.

By the oddness of
\{\mathcal{V}_{i}\}
on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{j},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle B_{p}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3},\mathcal{V}_{j}^{4}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+3B_{p}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{3},\rho_{*}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

(8.2)

\displaystyle+2A_{p}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2},\rho_{*}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

for all
1\leq j\leq\nu
, where
\mathcal{N}_{j}
is given by (
5.9
). By (
8.1
) and (
8.2
), we have

\displaystyle-\left\langle f,\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{2,j},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{j},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\left\langle\mathcal{L}_{j,ex}(\rho_{*}),\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{2,ex},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

(8.3)

\displaystyle+\left\langle\mathcal{N}-\mathcal{N}_{j},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{2,i},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}.

As in the proof of Proposition
4.1
, the rest of the proof is to estimate every terms in (
8.3
).

Step. 1
The estimate of
\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{2,j},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By (
3.19
),

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{2,j},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}p\left((\alpha_{j}^{*})^{p-1}-1\right)\left\|\Psi_{j}^{p-1}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\right\|_{L^{1}}.

Step. 2
The estimate of
\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{2,i},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By (
3.19
), Lemma
4.3
and Propositions
5.1
and
6.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{2,i},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\beta_{*}^{2}\left|(\alpha_{i}^{*})^{p-1}-1\right|\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{\frac{3p-1}{2}},\Psi_{j}^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Step. 3
The estimate of
\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{2,ex},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By (
4.30
), Lemma
4.3
and Propositions
5.1
and
6.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mathcal{R}_{2,ex},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\beta_{*}^{2}\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{2p-1},\mathcal{U}_{i}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{B}_{j})}=o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Step. 4
The estimate of
\left\langle\mathcal{L}_{j,ex}(\rho_{*}),\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By (
3.17
) and
(i)
and
(ii)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{L}_{j,ex}(\rho_{*}),\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p\left\langle\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-1}-\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-1}\right)\rho_{*},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\left\langle\beta_{*}\mathcal{U}_{**},\gamma_{2,ex}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}+\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right),

where
\mathcal{U}_{**}=\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\Psi_{j}^{\frac{3(p-1)}{2}}\mathcal{U}_{j}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}}+\mathcal{U}^{\frac{3p-1}{2}}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\cup_{i=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{B}_{i}}
.
By Lemmas
4.3
,
4.6
and Proposition
6.1
,

\displaystyle\beta_{*}\left\langle\mathcal{U}_{**},\gamma_{2,ex}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\beta_{*}^{2}Q\left\langle\Psi_{j}^{\frac{3p-1-2\sigma}{2}},\mathcal{U}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{B}_{j})},\quad p\geq\frac{7}{3},\\
&\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\beta_{*}^{2}Q\left\langle\Psi_{j}^{3p-4},\mathcal{U}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{B}_{j})},\quad 1<p<\frac{7}{3}\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

By Lemma
4.3
,
(iii)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
and Proposition
6.1
,

\displaystyle\beta_{*}\left\langle\mathcal{U}_{**},\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\beta_{*}^{3}Q+\left\{\begin{aligned} &\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\beta_{*}^{4}Q\left\langle\Psi_{j}^{\frac{3p-1-2\sigma}{2}}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}},\mathcal{U}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}},\quad p\geq 3,\\
&\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\beta_{*}^{4}Q\left\langle\Psi_{j}^{3p-4}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{j}},\mathcal{U}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}},\quad 1<p<3\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

By Lemma
4.3
and Proposition
6.1
,

\displaystyle\beta_{*}\left\langle\mathcal{U}_{**},\left|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right|\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\beta_{*}\left\|\Psi_{i}^{\frac{3(p-1)}{2}}\mathcal{U}_{i}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{B}_{j})}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|=o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Summarizing the above estimates, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{L}_{j,ex}(\rho_{*}),\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o(\beta_{*}^{4})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Step. 5
The estimate of
\left\langle\mathcal{N}-\mathcal{N}_{j},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

Similar to (
5.12
), by the oddness of
w_{j,l}
on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{N}-\mathcal{N}_{j},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}A_{p}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\left(\mathcal{V}_{i}^{2}+2\mathcal{V}_{i}\rho_{*}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}B_{p}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\left(\mathcal{V}_{i}^{3}+\mathcal{V}_{i}^{2}\rho_{*}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+2A_{p}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}\rho_{*}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\mathcal{B}_{j}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+B_{p}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\left(\mathcal{V}_{j}^{3}+3\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\rho_{*}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\mathcal{B}_{j}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}|\rho_{*}|\Psi_{i}^{\frac{3p-5}{2}}\mathcal{U}_{i}+\beta_{*}^{2}\Psi_{i}^{2p-2}\mathcal{U}_{i}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\left\langle\mathcal{O}\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\mathcal{V}^{2}+\beta_{*}|\rho_{*}|\mathcal{U}^{\frac{3(p-1)}{2}}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\left(\cup_{j=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{B}_{j}\right)},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\left\langle\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{rem},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{0},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}.

Step. 5.1
The estimate of
\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{i}^{2}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By Lemma
4.3
and Proposition
6.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{i}^{2}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|\lesssim\beta_{*}^{3}\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{2p-1},\Psi_{j}^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Step. 5.2
The estimate of
\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{i}^{3}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By (
4.2
), we also have

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{i}^{3}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{i}^{2}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Step. 5.3
The estimate of
\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{i}\rho_{*}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{i}\rho_{*}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=\left\langle\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\rho_{**}^{\perp}+\gamma_{ex}+\gamma_{*}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}.

By Lemma
4.3
,
(i)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
and Proposition
6.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\gamma_{ex}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\beta_{*}^{2}Q\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{\frac{3p-1-2\sigma}{2}},\Psi_{j}^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{B}_{i})},\quad p\geq 3,\\
&\beta_{*}^{2}Q\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{\frac{5p-7}{2}},\Psi_{j}^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{B}_{i})},\quad 1<p<3\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

By Lemma
4.3
,
(ii)
and
(iii)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
and Propositions
5.1
and
6.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\gamma_{*}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\beta_{*}^{4}\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{\frac{3p-1-2\sigma}{2}},\Psi_{j}^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{B}_{i})}

\displaystyle+o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(\beta_{*}^{4})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

By Lemma
4.3
and Proposition
6.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{i}\mathcal{V}_{j}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\beta_{*}^{2}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|\left(\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{3p-3},\Psi_{j}^{p+1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{B}_{i})}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Summarizing the above estimates, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{i}\rho_{*}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Step. 5.4
The estimate of
\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{i}^{2}\rho_{*}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By (
4.2
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{i}^{2}\rho_{*}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{i}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{i}\rho_{*}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Step. 5.5
The estimate of
\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}\rho_{*}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\mathcal{B}_{j}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}\rho_{*}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\mathcal{B}_{j}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\mathcal{B}_{j}},\rho_{**}^{\perp}+\gamma_{1,ex}+\gamma_{*}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}.

By Lemmas
4.3
,
4.4
and Proposition
6.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\mathcal{B}_{j}},\gamma_{1,ex}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\beta_{*}^{2}Q\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{1-\sigma},\Psi_{j}^{2p-1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{B}_{i})},\quad p\geq 3,\\
&\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\beta_{*}^{2}Q\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{p-2},\Psi_{j}^{2p-1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{B}_{i})},\quad 1<p<3\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

By Lemma
4.3
,
(ii)
and
(iii)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
and Propositions
5.1
and
6.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\mathcal{B}_{j}},\gamma_{*}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1;i\not=j}^{\nu}\beta_{*}^{4}\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{1-\sigma},\Psi_{j}^{2p-1}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{B}_{i})}

\displaystyle+o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

By Lemma
4.3
and Proposition
6.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\mathcal{B}_{j}},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\beta_{*}^{2}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|\left(\int_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\mathcal{B}_{j}}\Psi_{j}^{4p-2}d\mu\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Summarizing the above estimates, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}\rho_{*}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\mathcal{B}_{j}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o(\beta_{*}^{4})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Step. 5.6
The estimate of
\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\left(\mathcal{V}_{j}^{3}+3\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\rho_{*}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\mathcal{B}_{j}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By Lemma
4.3
and
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\left(\mathcal{V}_{j}^{3}+3\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\rho_{*}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\mathcal{B}_{j}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{3}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\mathcal{B}_{j}},\gamma_{2,ex}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle 3\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{3}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\mathcal{B}_{j}},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+o(\beta_{*}^{4}).

By Lemmas
4.3
and
4.6
,
\left|\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{3}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\mathcal{B}_{j}},\gamma_{2,ex}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|=o(\beta_{*}^{4})
.
By Lemma
4.3
and
(iii)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{3}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\mathcal{B}_{j}},\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|=o(\beta_{*}^{4}).

By Lemma
4.3
and Proposition
6.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{3}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\mathcal{B}_{j}},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\beta_{*}^{3}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|\left(\int_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\mathcal{B}_{j}}\Psi_{j}^{5p-3}d\mu\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Summarizing the above estimates, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3}\left(\mathcal{V}_{j}^{3}+3\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\rho_{*}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\mathcal{B}_{j}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Step. 5.7
The estimate of
\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\beta_{*}\rho_{*}\Psi_{i}^{\frac{3p-5}{2}}\mathcal{U}_{i}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\beta_{*}\rho_{*}\Psi_{i}^{\frac{3p-5}{2}}\mathcal{U}_{i}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\beta_{*}^{2}\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{2p-2}\mathcal{U}_{i}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\left|\rho_{**}^{\perp}+\gamma_{ex}+\gamma_{*}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}\right|\right\rangle_{L^{2}}.

By Lemma
4.3
,
(i)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
, and Proposition
6.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\beta_{*}^{2}\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{2p-2}\mathcal{U}_{i}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\gamma_{ex}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\beta_{*}^{2}Q\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{2p-1-\sigma},\mathcal{U}_{i}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{B}_{i,+})},\quad p\geq 3,\\
&\beta_{*}^{2}Q\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{3p-4},\mathcal{U}_{i}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{B}_{i,+})},\quad 1<p<3\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

By Lemma
4.3
,
(ii)
and
(iii)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
and Propositions
5.1
and
6.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\beta_{*}^{2}\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{2p-2}\mathcal{U}_{i}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\gamma_{*}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\beta_{*}^{4}Q+o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

By Lemma
4.3
and Proposition
6.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\beta_{*}^{2}\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{2p-2}\mathcal{U}_{i}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\beta_{*}^{2}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|\left(\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{4p-4},\mathcal{U}_{i}^{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{B}_{i})}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Summarizing the above estimates, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\beta_{*}\rho_{*}\Psi_{i}^{\frac{3p-5}{2}}\mathcal{U}_{i}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Step. 5.8
The estimates of

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\beta_{*}^{2}\Psi_{i}^{2p-2}\mathcal{U}_{i}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\quad\text{and}\quad\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\mathcal{V}^{2}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\cup_{i=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}.

By Lemma
4.3
and Proposition
6.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\beta_{*}^{2}\Psi_{i}^{2p-2}\mathcal{U}_{i}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\beta_{*}^{3}\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\Psi_{i}^{\frac{5p-3}{2}},\mathcal{U}_{i}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathcal{B}_{i})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right)

and

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\mathcal{V}^{2}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\cup_{i=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\beta_{*}^{3}\int_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\cup_{i=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{B}_{i}}\mathcal{U}^{\frac{5p-1}{2}}d\mu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Step. 5.9
The estimate of
\left\langle\beta_{*}\rho_{*}\mathcal{U}^{\frac{3(p-1)}{2}}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\cup_{i=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\beta_{*}\rho_{*}\mathcal{U}^{\frac{3(p-1)}{2}}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\cup_{i=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\beta_{*}^{2}\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{2p-1}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\cup_{i=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{B}_{i}},\gamma_{ex}+\gamma_{*}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\beta_{*}^{2}\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{2p-1}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\cup_{i=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{B}_{i}},\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}.

Lemma
4.3
,
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
and Propositions
5.1
and
6.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\beta_{*}\rho_{*}\mathcal{U}^{\frac{3(p-1)}{2}}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\cup_{i=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{B}_{i}},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\beta_{*}^{4}\left\|\mathcal{U}^{2p-\sigma}\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{C}\backslash\cup_{i=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{B}_{i})}+\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}

\displaystyle+\beta_{*}^{2}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|\left\|\mathcal{U}^{2p-1}\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{C}\backslash\cup_{i=1}^{\nu}\mathcal{B}_{i})}+o(\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Step. 5.10
The estimate of
\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{0},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By (
4.2
), (
5.1
) and Lemma
5.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{0},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|\lesssim\beta_{*}\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\Psi_{j}^{\frac{p+1}{2}},\gamma_{ex}^{2}+\left|\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}+\gamma_{*}\right|^{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\beta_{*}\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2}.

By Lemma
4.3
,
(i)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
, and Propositions
6.1
and
7.1
,

\displaystyle\beta_{*}\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{p-2}\Psi_{j}^{\frac{p+1}{2}},\gamma_{ex}^{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\beta_{*}Q^{2}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{j}}\Psi_{j}^{\frac{3p+1-4\sigma}{2}}d\mu,\quad p\geq 3,\\
&\beta_{*}Q^{2}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{j}}\Psi_{j}^{\frac{7p-11}{2}}d\mu,\quad 1<p<3\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

By Lemma
4.3
,
(ii)
and
(iii)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
and Propositions
5.1
and
6.1
,

\displaystyle\beta_{*}\left\langle\mathcal{U}^{2}\Psi_{j}^{\frac{p+1}{2}},\left|\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}+\gamma_{*}\right|^{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\beta_{*}\left(\beta_{*}^{2}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right)^{2}+o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Summarizing the above estimates, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{N}_{0},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

Step. 5.11
The estimate of
\left\langle\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{rem},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}
.

By Lemma
2.1
and Propositions
5.1
,
6.1
and
7.1
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{rem},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|\lesssim\beta_{*}\left(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{1+\varepsilon}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right)=o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

By summarizing the estimates from Step. 5.1 to Step. 5.11, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{N}-\mathcal{N}_{j},\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\|\rho_{**}^{\perp}\|^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right).

The conclusion follows from the estimates from Step. 1 to Step. 5 and Proposition
7.1
.
∎

With Proposition
8.1
in hands, we can finally estimate
\beta_{*}
.

Proposition 8.2
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
a<0
and
b=b_{FS}(a)
. Then we have
\beta_{*}\lesssim\|f\|_{H^{-1}}^{\frac{1}{3}}
.

Proof.

By Lemmas
4.3
,
4.9
and
4.10
,
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
and Propositions
5.1
,
6.1
and
7.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2},\rho_{*}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2},\gamma_{ex}+\gamma_{*}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}+\rho_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

(8.4)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2},\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}(\gamma_{1,i}+\alpha_{i,0}^{**}\Psi_{i})+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}).

We write
\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\gamma_{1,i}+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led}=\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\overline{\alpha}_{j,*}\Psi_{j}+\overline{\gamma}_{**}^{\perp}

such that
\left\langle\Psi_{j},\overline{\gamma}_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle
for all
1\leq j\leq\nu
. Then by (
4.38
) and
(i)
of
(1)
of Proposition
4.1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}(\overline{\alpha}_{i,*}+\alpha_{i,0}^{**}\Psi_{i})+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}(\gamma_{1,i}+\alpha_{i,0}^{**}\Psi_{i})+\gamma_{\mathcal{N},led},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle

(8.5)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left\langle\gamma_{1,ex},\Psi_{j}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(Q).

It follows from (
8.4
) and Propositions
6.1
and
7.1
that

\displaystyle\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2},\rho_{*}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2},\overline{\gamma}_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+o(\beta_{*}^{4}+\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}).

(8.6)

By (
4.25
), (
4.32
) and Lemma
7.1
, we know that
\overline{\gamma}_{**}^{\perp}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{L}(\overline{\gamma}_{**}^{\perp})=\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{1,**}-\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\Psi_{i}^{p-1}\left(c_{1,j,i}\partial_{t}\Psi_{i}+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\varsigma_{1,j,i,l}w_{i,l}\right),\quad\text{in }\mathcal{C},\\
&\langle\Psi_{j},\overline{\gamma}_{**}^{\perp}\rangle=\langle\partial_{t}\Psi_{j},\overline{\gamma}_{**}^{\perp}\rangle=\langle w_{j,l},\overline{\gamma}_{**}^{\perp}\rangle=0\quad\text{for all }1\leq j\leq\nu\text{ and all }1\leq l\leq d,\end{aligned}\right.

(8.7)

where

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{1,**}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{led}+\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}\left(\left(\alpha_{l}^{*}\right)^{p}-\alpha_{l}^{*}-\overline{\alpha}_{l,*}\left(1-p\left(\alpha_{l}^{*}\right)^{p-1}\right)\right)\Psi_{l}^{p}

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=1}^{\nu}p\overline{\alpha}_{l,*}\left(\mathcal{U}^{p-1}-\left(\Psi_{l}^{*}\right)^{p-1}\right)\Psi_{l}

with
\mathcal{N}_{led}
given by (
4.35
). Since by (
4.39
), (
8.5
) and Propositions
5.1
,
6.1
and
7.1
, we have
\sum_{i=1}^{\nu}\left|\overline{\alpha}_{i,*}\right|=\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}^{2}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right)
. Thus, by the orthogonal conditions of
\overline{\gamma}_{**}^{\perp}
, multiplying (
8.7
) with
\overline{\gamma}_{**}^{\perp}
on both sides and integrating by parts, Lemma
4.3
and Propositions
6.1
and
7.1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{L}(\overline{\gamma}_{**}^{\perp})-\mathcal{N}_{led},\overline{\gamma}_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=o\left(\beta_{*}^{4}+\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\|f\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}\right).

(8.8)

Since
Q\to 0
and
\beta_{*}\to 0
as
\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\to 0
, by Lemma
4.3
, Propositions
5.1
,
6.1
and
7.1
, it is easy to see that

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{L}(\overline{\gamma}_{**}^{\perp})-\mathcal{N}_{led},\overline{\gamma}_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left\langle\mathcal{L}_{j}(\overline{\gamma}_{**}^{\perp})-\frac{p(p-1)}{2}\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2},\overline{\gamma}_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+o(\beta_{*}^{4})+\mathcal{O}(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}})

which, together with (
8.6
), (
8.8
) and Propositions
5.1
,
6.1
,
7.1
and
8.1
, implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left(\left\langle f,\mathcal{V}_{j}\right\rangle_{H^{1}}-\frac{\left\langle\Psi_{j}^{p-1},\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}}{\alpha_{j}^{*}\|\Psi\|_{L^{p+1}}^{p+1}}\left\langle f,\Psi_{j}\right\rangle_{H^{1}}\right)

(8.9)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}2\left\langle\mathcal{L}_{j}(\overline{\gamma}_{**}^{\perp})-p(p-1)\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-2}\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2},\overline{\gamma}_{**}^{\perp}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=1}^{\nu}\left(\frac{p^{2}(p-1)(\alpha_{j}^{*})^{p-3}\left(\left\langle\Psi_{j}^{p-1},\mathcal{V}_{j}^{2}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right)^{2}}{\|\Psi\|_{L^{p+1}}^{p+1}}+\frac{p(p-1)(p-2)}{6}\left\langle\left(\Psi_{j}^{*}\right)^{p-3},\mathcal{V}_{j}^{4}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+o(\beta_{*}^{4})+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta_{*}\|f\|_{H^{-1}}+\|f\|_{H^{-1}}^{2}\right).

The conclusion then follows from applying the estimates in
[
53
, Section 4.3]
and the orthogonal conditions of
\overline{\gamma}_{**}^{\perp}
given in (
8.7
) into (
8.9
).
∎

We are now ready to given the proof of
(a)
of Theorem
1.3
.

Proof of
(a)
of Theorem
1.3
:
The conclusions for
\nu\geq 2
follows immediately from Lemma
3.1
and Propositions
5.1
,
6.1
,
7.1
,
8.1
and
8.2
. For
\nu=1
, there is no interaction between bubbles, that is, we have
Q=0
. Thus, the conclusion for
\nu=1
follows from Lemma
3.1
and Propositions
5.1
,
7.1
,
8.1
and
8.2

\Box

9.
Optimal example and proof of
(b)
of Theorem
1.3

Let
R>0
be a sufficiently large parameter and
\beta>0
is a sufficiently small parameter. We shall use the function, given by

\displaystyle v=\Psi+\Psi_{R}+\beta(w_{d}+w_{R,d}):=\Gamma_{R}+\beta\Phi_{R}

to construct a optimal example of the stability stated in Theorem
1.3
, where

\Psi_{R}=\Psi(t-R)
and, as above,
w_{d}=\Psi^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\theta_{d}
and
w_{R,d}=w_{d}(t-R)
. It is easy to see that

\displaystyle\frac{3}{2}\left(S_{FS}^{-1}\right)^{\frac{p+1}{p-1}}<\|v\|_{H^{1}}^{2}<\frac{5}{2}\left(S_{FS}^{-1}\right)^{\frac{p+1}{p-1}}.

Moreover, since
\Psi(t)
is the unique positive solution of (
2.5
) for
d\geq 2
,
a<0
and
b=b_{FS}(a)
, by Lemmas
2.1
and
4.2
,

\displaystyle\Xi

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle-\Delta_{\theta}v-\partial_{t}^{2}v+\Lambda_{FS}v-v^{p}

(9.1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Psi^{p}+\Psi_{R}^{p}+p\beta\left(\Psi^{p-1}w_{d}+\Psi_{R}^{p-1}w_{R,d}\right)-\left(\Gamma_{R}+\beta\Phi_{R}\right)^{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Psi^{p}+\Psi_{R}^{p}-\Gamma_{R}^{p}+p\beta\left(\left(\Psi^{p-1}-\Gamma_{R}^{p-1}\right)w_{d}+\left(\Psi_{R}^{p-1}-\Gamma_{R}^{p-1}\right)w_{R,d}\right)

\displaystyle-\left(A_{p}\beta^{2}\Psi^{p-2}w_{d}^{2}+B_{p}\beta^{3}\Psi^{p-3}w_{d}^{3}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}}-\left(\beta^{2}\Gamma_{R}^{p-3}\Phi_{R}^{2}\left(A_{p}\Gamma_{R}+B_{p}\beta\Phi_{R}\right)\right)\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\left(\mathcal{B}\cup\mathcal{B}_{R}\right)}

\displaystyle-\left(A_{p}\beta^{2}\Psi_{R}^{p-2}w_{R,d}^{2}+B_{p}\beta^{3}\Psi_{R}^{p-3}w_{R,d}^{3}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{R}}+\Xi_{rem}

where
A_{p}=\frac{p(p-1)}{2}
,
B_{p}=\frac{p(p-1)(p-2)}{6}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}=\left[-\frac{R}{2},\frac{R}{2}\right]\times\mathbb{S}^{d-1},\quad\mathcal{B}_{R}=\left[\frac{R}{2},\frac{3R}{2}\right]\times\mathbb{S}^{d-1}

and

\displaystyle\Xi_{rem}=\mathcal{O}\left(\beta^{2}\left(\Psi^{2(p-1)}\Psi_{R}\chi_{\mathcal{B}}+\Psi_{R}^{2(p-1)}\Psi\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{R}}\right)+\beta^{4}\Gamma_{R}^{4}\right).

We denote

\displaystyle\Xi_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\Gamma_{R}^{p}-\Psi^{p}-\Psi_{R}^{p}\right)+p\beta\left(\left(\Gamma_{R}^{p-1}-\Psi^{p-1}\right)w_{d}+\left(\Gamma_{R}^{p-1}-\Psi_{R}^{p-1}\right)w_{R,d}\right)

(9.2)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\Xi_{1,1}+\beta\Xi_{1,2}

and

\displaystyle\Xi_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{p}\beta^{2}\left(\Psi^{p-2}w_{d}^{2}\chi_{\mathcal{B}}+\Psi_{R}^{p-2}w_{R,d}^{2}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{R}}+\Gamma_{R}^{p-2}\Phi_{R}^{2}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\left(\mathcal{B}\cup\mathcal{B}_{R}\right)}\right)

(9.3)

\displaystyle+B_{p}\beta^{3}\left(\Psi^{p-3}w_{d}^{3}\chi_{\mathcal{B}}+\Psi_{R}^{p-3}w_{R,d}^{3}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{R}}+\Gamma_{R}^{p-3}\Phi_{R}^{3}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\left(\mathcal{B}\cup\mathcal{B}_{R}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\beta^{2}\Xi_{2,1}+\beta^{3}\Xi_{2,2}.

Applying Lemmas
4.4
,
4.6
and
4.7
, we immediately have the following.

Lemma 9.1
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
a<0
and
b=b_{FS}(a)
. Then the following equation

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &-\Delta_{\theta}\varrho_{i,j}-\partial_{t}^{2}\varrho_{i,j}+\Lambda_{FS}\varrho_{i,j}-p\Gamma_{R}^{p-1}\varrho_{i,j}=\Xi_{i,j}+\vartheta_{i,j},\quad\text{in }\mathcal{C},\\
&\langle\partial_{t}\Psi,\varrho_{i,j}\rangle=\langle\partial_{t}\Psi_{R},\varrho_{i,j}\rangle=\langle w_{l},\varrho_{i,j}\rangle=\langle w_{R,l},\varrho_{i,j}\rangle=0\text{ for all }1\leq l\leq d,\end{aligned}\right.

(9.4)

is uniquely solvable, where
\Xi_{i,j}
is given by (
9.2
) and (
9.3
), and

\displaystyle\vartheta_{i,j}=\Psi^{p-1}\left(c_{i,j}\partial_{t}\Psi+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\varsigma_{i,j,l}w_{l}\right)+\Psi_{R}^{p-1}\left(c_{R,i,j}\partial_{t}\Psi_{R}+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\varsigma_{R,i,j,l}w_{R,l}\right)

(9.5)

with
c_{i,j},c_{R,i,j}
and
\{\varsigma_{i,j,l}\}
and
\{\varsigma_{R,i,j,l}\}
being chosen such that the right hand side of the equation (
9.4
) is orthogonal to
\Psi^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi
,
\Psi_{R}^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi_{R}
,
\left\{\Psi^{p-1}w_{l}\right\}
and
\left\{\Psi_{R}^{p-1}w_{R,l}\right\}
in
L^{2}(\mathcal{C})
. Moreover,
\varrho_{1,1}
is even on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
and
\varrho_{1,2}
is odd on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
with

\displaystyle 1\gtrsim\left\{\begin{aligned} &\|\varrho_{1,1}\|_{\sharp},\quad p\geq 3,\\
&\|\varrho_{1,1}\|_{\natural,1,*},\quad 1<p<3,\end{aligned}\right.\quad\text{and}\quad 1\gtrsim\left\{\begin{aligned} &\|\varrho_{1,2}\|_{\sharp},\quad p\geq\frac{7}{3},\\
&\|\varrho_{1,2}\|_{\natural,2,*},\quad 1<p<\frac{7}{3},\end{aligned}\right.

while,
\varrho_{2,1}
is even on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
and
\varrho_{2,2}
is odd on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
with

\displaystyle 1\gtrsim\sup_{(t,\theta)\in\mathcal{C}}\frac{|\varrho_{2,1}|+|\varrho_{2,2}|}{\Psi^{1-\sigma}+\Psi_{R}^{1-\sigma}}

and
\sigma>0
is chosen to satisfy
\beta^{2}\leq\frac{1}{8}\Gamma_{R}^{\sigma}
in
\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{*}=[-R,2R]\times\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
.

Let
\varrho_{*}=\varrho_{1,1}+\beta\varrho_{1,2}+\beta^{2}\varrho_{2,1}+\beta^{3}\varrho_{2,2}
.
Then we have the following.

Proposition 9.1
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
a<0
and
b=b_{FS}(a)
. Then

\displaystyle\|\varrho_{*}\|\sim\beta^{2}+\left\{\begin{aligned} &Q_{R},\quad p>2,\\
&Q_{R}\left|\log Q_{R}\right|,\quad p=2,\\
&Q_{R}^{\frac{p}{2}},\quad 1<p<2,\end{aligned}\right.

(9.6)

where
Q_{R}=e^{-\sqrt{\Lambda_{FS}}R}
.

Proof.

By using the test functions

\displaystyle\widetilde{\varrho}_{R}(t)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1,\quad\frac{R}{2}-3\leq t\leq\frac{R}{2}-2,\\
&0,\quad t\leq\frac{R}{2}-4\text{ or }t\geq\frac{R}{2}-1.\end{aligned}\right.

for
1<p<2
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\varrho}_{R}(t)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1,\quad\frac{R}{4}\leq t\leq\frac{R}{2}-2,\\
&0,\quad t\leq\frac{R}{4}-1\text{ or }t\geq\frac{R}{2}-1.\end{aligned}\right.

for
p=2
and

\displaystyle\widehat{\varrho}_{R}(t)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1,\quad T_{*}\leq t\leq T_{*}+1,\\
&0,\quad t\leq T_{*}-1\text{ or }t\geq T_{*}+2,\end{aligned}\right.

with
T_{*}>0
sufficiently large for
p>2
to (
9.4
), as that in the proof of
[
78
, Proposition 6.2]
, we can show that

\displaystyle\|\varrho_{1,1}\|\gtrsim\left\{\begin{aligned} &Q_{R},\quad p>2,\\
&Q_{R}\left|\log Q_{R}\right|,\quad p=2,\\
&Q_{R}^{\frac{p}{2}},\quad 1<p<2,\end{aligned}\right.

which, together with (
9.2
), Lemma
9.1
and multiplying (
9.4
) of
\varrho_{1,1}
with
\varrho_{1,1}
on both sides and integrating by parts, implies that

\displaystyle\|\varrho_{1,1}\|\sim\left\{\begin{aligned} &Q_{R},\quad p>2,\\
&Q_{R}\left|\log Q_{R}\right|,\quad p=2,\\
&Q_{R}^{\frac{p}{2}},\quad 1<p<2.\end{aligned}\right.

Similar to (
4.54
), by (
9.2
), Lemma
9.1
and multiplying (
9.4
) of
\varrho_{1,2}
with
\varrho_{1,2}
on both sides and integrating by parts, we also have

\displaystyle\|\varrho_{1,2}\|\lesssim\left\{\begin{aligned} &Q_{R},\quad p>2,\\
&Q_{R}\left|\log Q_{R}\right|,\quad p=2,\\
&Q_{R}^{\frac{p}{2}},\quad 1<p<2.\end{aligned}\right.

By (
9.3
), Lemma
9.1
and multiplying (
9.4
) of
\varrho_{2,2}
with
\varrho_{2,2}
on both sides and integrating by parts, it is also easy to see that
\|\varrho_{2,2}\|\lesssim 1
. It remains to estimate
\|\varrho_{2,1}\|
. By (
9.3
), Lemma
9.1
and multiplying (
9.4
) of
\varrho_{2,1}
with
\varrho_{2,1}
on both sides and integrating by parts, it is also easy to see that
\|\varrho_{2,1}\|\lesssim 1
. For the lower bound of
\|\varrho_{2,1}\|
, we recall that the spherical harmonics on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, denoted by
\{\mathcal{Y}_{j,l}\}
with
j=0,1,2,\cdots
and
l=1,2,\cdots,l_{j}
for some
l_{j}\in\mathbb{N}
, form an orthogonal basic of
L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})
with span
{}_{1\leq l\leq l_{j}}\{\mathcal{Y}_{j,l}\}
forming the eigenspace of the
j
th eigenvalue of
-\Delta_{\theta}
on
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\right)
, where
\Delta_{\theta}
is the Laplace-Beltrami operator on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
. Moreover, it is well known that the eigenvalues of
-\Delta_{\theta}
on
L^{2}\left(\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\right)
are given by
j(j+d-2)
.
The first eigenvalue
0
is simple with eigenfunctions
\mathcal{Y}_{0,1}=1
, the eigenfunctions of the second eigenvalue
d-1
are precisely
\mathcal{Y}_{1,l}=\theta_{l}
for
1\leq l\leq d
. It is also well known that
\mathcal{Y}_{2,d}=\theta_{d}^{2}-\frac{1}{d}
is a spherical harmonic on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
with degree
2
(cf.
[
73
, (2.6)]
or
[
53
, (4.9)]
). Now, by (
9.3
), Lemma
9.1
and multiplying (
9.4
) of
\varrho_{2,1}
with
\mathcal{Y}_{2,d}
on both sides and integrating by parts,we have

\displaystyle\|\varrho_{2,1}\|\gtrsim\left\langle\varrho_{2,1},\mathcal{Y}_{2,d}\right\rangle-p\left\langle\Gamma_{R}\varrho_{2,1},\mathcal{Y}_{2,d}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=\left\langle\Xi_{2,1},\mathcal{Y}_{2,d}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\gtrsim\left\|\Psi^{p-2}\mathcal{Y}_{2,d}^{2}\right\|_{L^{1}}.

Thus, by
\varrho_{*}=\varrho_{1,1}+\beta\varrho_{1,2}+\beta^{2}\varrho_{2,1}+\beta^{3}\varrho_{2,2}
, we have the desired estimate of
\|\varrho\|
given by (
9.7
).
∎

We define

\displaystyle f_{*}:=-\Delta_{\theta}(\varrho_{*}+v)-\partial_{t}^{2}(\varrho_{*}+v)+\Lambda_{FS}(\varrho_{*}+v)-(v+\varrho_{*})^{p}.

(9.7)

Then by (
9.1
) and Lemma
9.1
,

\displaystyle f_{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(-\Delta_{\theta}\varrho_{*}-\partial_{t}^{2}\varrho_{*}+\Lambda_{FS}\varrho_{*}-p\Gamma_{R}^{p-1}\varrho_{*}\right)+\Psi^{p}+\Psi_{R}^{p}+p\beta\left(\Psi^{p-1}w_{d}+\Psi_{R}^{p-1}w_{R,d}\right)

(9.8)

\displaystyle+p\Gamma_{R}^{p-1}\varrho_{*}-(\Gamma_{R}+\beta\Phi_{R}+\varrho_{*})^{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vartheta_{1,1}+\beta\vartheta_{1,2}+\beta^{2}\vartheta_{2,1}+\beta^{3}\vartheta_{2,2}+\Xi_{1,1}+\beta\Xi_{1,2}+\beta^{2}\Xi_{2,1}+\beta^{3}\Xi_{2,2}

\displaystyle+\Psi^{p}+\Psi_{R}^{p}-\Gamma_{R}^{p}+p\beta\left(\left(\Psi^{p-1}-\Gamma_{R}\right)w_{d}+\left(\Psi_{R}^{p-1}-\Gamma_{R}\right)w_{R,d}\right)

\displaystyle+p\Gamma_{R}^{p-1}(\varrho_{*}+\beta\Phi_{R})+\Gamma_{R}^{p}-(\Gamma_{R}+\beta\Phi_{R}+\varrho_{*})^{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vartheta_{1,1}+\beta\vartheta_{1,2}+\beta^{2}\vartheta_{2,1}+\beta^{3}\vartheta_{2,2}+\beta^{2}\Xi_{2,1}+\beta^{3}\Xi_{2,2}-\mathcal{N}_{\varrho_{*}},

where

\vartheta_{i,j}
is given by (
9.5
) and by Lemmas
4.1
,
4.2
and
5.1
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\varrho_{*}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{p}\Gamma_{P}^{p-2}\left((\beta\Phi_{R})^{2}+2\beta\Phi_{R}\varrho_{*}+\varrho_{*}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+B_{p}\Gamma_{P}^{p-3}\left((\beta\Phi_{R})^{3}+3(\beta\Phi_{R})^{2}\varrho_{*}+3(\beta\Phi_{R})\varrho_{*}^{2}+\varrho_{*}^{3}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\left(\Gamma_{R}^{p-4\sigma}\left(\beta+Q_{R}^{\frac{2\wedge p}{2}}\left|\log Q_{R}\right|\right)^{4}+\left(\beta^{2}\Gamma_{R}^{1-\sigma}\right)^{1+\varepsilon}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{*}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta^{2}\Xi_{2,1}+\beta^{3}\Xi_{2,2}+\mathcal{N}_{\varrho_{*},rem},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\varrho_{*},rem}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2A_{p}\beta\left(\Psi^{p-2}w_{d}\chi_{\mathcal{B}}+\Psi_{R}^{p-2}w_{R,d}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{R}}+\Gamma_{R}^{p-2}\Phi_{R}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\left(\mathcal{B}\cup\mathcal{B}_{R}\right)}\right)\varrho_{*}

\displaystyle+3B_{p}\beta^{2}\left(\Psi^{p-3}w_{d}^{2}\chi_{\mathcal{B}}+\Psi_{R}^{p-3}w_{R,d}^{2}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{R}}\right)\varrho_{*}

\displaystyle+2\beta A_{p}\left((\Gamma_{R}^{p-2}\Phi_{R}-\Psi^{p-2}w_{d})\chi_{\mathcal{B}}+(\Gamma_{R}^{p-2}\Phi_{R}-\Psi_{R}^{p-2}w_{R,d}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{R}})\right)\varrho_{*}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta^{2}\left(\left(\Psi^{2p-2}\Psi_{R}+\Psi^{2p-3}\Psi_{R}\varrho_{*}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}}+\left(\Psi_{R}^{2p-2}\Psi+\Psi_{R}^{2p-3}\Psi\varrho_{*}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{R}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\left(\Gamma_{R}^{p-4\sigma}\left(\beta+Q_{R}^{\frac{2\wedge p}{2}}\left|\log Q_{R}\right|\right)^{4}+\left(\beta^{2}\Gamma_{R}^{1-\sigma}\right)^{1+\varepsilon}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{*}}\right).

By Lemmas
4.11
and
9.1
, we immediately have the following.

Lemma 9.2
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
a<0
and
b=b_{FS}(a)
. Then the following equation,

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &-\Delta_{\theta}\varrho_{1,1,*}-\partial_{t}^{2}\varrho_{1,1,*}+\Lambda_{FS}\varrho_{1,1,*}-p\Gamma_{R}^{p-1}\varrho_{1,1,*}=\Xi_{1,1,*}+\vartheta_{1,1,*},\quad\text{in }\mathcal{C},\\
&\langle\partial_{t}\Psi,\varrho_{1,1,*}\rangle=\langle\partial_{t}\Psi_{R},\varrho_{1,1,*}\rangle=\langle w_{l},\varrho_{1,1,*}\rangle=\langle w_{R,l},\varrho_{1,1,*}\rangle=0\text{ for all }1\leq l\leq d,\end{aligned}\right.

(9.9)

is uniquely solvable, where
\Xi_{1,1,*}=2A_{p}\Gamma_{R}^{p-2}\Phi_{R}\varrho_{1,1}
and
and

\displaystyle\vartheta_{1,1,*}=\Psi^{p-1}\left(c_{1,1,*}\partial_{t}\Psi+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\varsigma_{1,1,*,l}w_{l}\right)+\Psi_{R}^{p-1}\left(c_{R,1,1,*}\partial_{t}\Psi_{R}+\sum_{l=1}^{d}\varsigma_{R,1,1,*,l}w_{R,l}\right)

with
c_{1,1,*},c_{R,1,1,*}
and
\{\varsigma_{1,1,*,l}\}
and
\{\varsigma_{R,1,1,*,l}\}
being chosen such that the right hand side of the equation (
9.9
) is orthogonal to
\Psi^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi
,
\Psi_{R}^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi_{R}
,
\left\{\Psi^{p-1}w_{l}\right\}
and
\left\{\Psi_{R}^{p-1}w_{R,l}\right\}
in
L^{2}(\mathcal{C})
. Moreover,
\varrho_{1,1,*}
is odd on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
with

\displaystyle 1\gtrsim\left\{\begin{aligned} &\|\varrho_{1,1,*}\|_{\sharp},\quad p\geq\frac{7}{3},\\
&\|\varrho_{1,1,*}\|_{\natural,2,*},\quad 1<p<\frac{7}{3}.\end{aligned}\right.

Let
\varrho=\varrho_{*}+\varrho_{1,1,*}
and define

\displaystyle f:=-\Delta_{\theta}(\varrho+v)-\partial_{t}^{2}(\varrho+v)+\Lambda_{FS}(\varrho+v)-(v+\varrho)^{p}.

(9.10)

Then by (
9.8
) and Lemma
9.2
,

\displaystyle f=\vartheta_{1,1}+\beta(\vartheta_{1,2}+\vartheta_{1,1,*})+\beta^{2}\vartheta_{2,1}+\beta^{3}\vartheta_{2,2}-\mathcal{N}_{\varrho_{*},rem,1},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\varrho_{*},rem,1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3B_{p}\beta^{2}\left(\Psi^{p-3}w_{d}^{2}\chi_{\mathcal{B}}+\Psi_{R}^{p-3}w_{R,d}^{2}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{R}}\right)\varrho_{*}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta^{2}\left(\left(\Psi^{2p-2}\Psi_{R}+\Psi^{2p-3}\Psi_{R}\varrho_{*}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}}+\left(\Psi_{R}^{2p-2}\Psi+\Psi_{R}^{2p-3}\Psi\varrho_{*}\right)\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{R}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\left(\Gamma_{R}^{p-4\sigma}\left(\beta+Q_{R}^{\frac{2\wedge p}{2}}\left|\log Q_{R}\right|\right)^{4}+\left(\beta^{2}\Gamma_{R}^{1-\sigma}\right)^{1+\varepsilon}\chi_{\mathcal{C}\backslash\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{*}}\right).

Proposition 9.2
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
a<0
and
b=b_{FS}(a)
. Then

\displaystyle\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\sim\beta^{3}+Q_{R},

where
Q_{R}=e^{-\sqrt{\Lambda_{FS}}R}
.

Proof.

For the sake of simplicity, we redenote
\vartheta_{1,2}+\vartheta_{1,1,*}
by
\vartheta_{1,2}
,
c_{1,2}+c_{1,1,*}
by
c_{1,2}
,
c_{R,1,2}+c_{R,1,1,*}
by
c_{R,1,2}
,
\varsigma_{1,2,l}+\varsigma_{1,1,*,l}
by
\varsigma_{1,2,l}
and
\varsigma_{R,1,2,l}+\varsigma_{R,1,1,*,l}
by
\varsigma_{R,1,2,l}
.
As in the proof of Lemma
4.10
, by Lemma
9.1
, the orthogonality of
\Psi^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi
and
\left\{\Psi^{p-1}w_{l}\right\}
in
L^{2}(\mathcal{C})
and the oddness of
w_{d}
on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,

\displaystyle-\left\langle\Psi^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi,\Xi_{i,j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=\left\|\Psi^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}c_{i,j}+\left\langle\Psi^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi,\Psi_{R}^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi_{R}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}c_{R,i,j}

and

\displaystyle-\left\langle\Psi_{R}^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi_{R},\Xi_{i,j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=\left\|\Psi^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}c_{R,i,j}+\left\langle\Psi^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi,\Psi_{R}^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi_{R}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}c_{i,j}

while for all
1\leq l\leq d
,

\displaystyle-\left\langle\Psi^{p-1}w_{j},\Xi_{i,j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=\sum_{l=1}^{d}\left(\left\langle\Psi^{p-1}w_{j},\Psi^{p-1}w_{l}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\varsigma_{i,j,l}+\left\langle\Psi^{p-1}w_{j},\Psi_{R}^{p-1}w_{R,i,j,l}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\varsigma_{R,i,j,l}\right)

and

\displaystyle-\left\langle\Psi_{R}^{p-1}w_{R,j},\Xi_{i,j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=\sum_{l=1}^{d}\left(\left\langle\Psi_{R}^{p-1}w_{R,j},\Psi^{p-1}w_{l}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\varsigma_{i,j,l}+\left\langle\Psi_{R}^{p-1}w_{R,j},\Psi_{R}^{p-1}w_{R,i,j,l}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\varsigma_{R,i,j,l}\right).

It follows from Lemma
4.3
that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &c_{i,j}=-B_{*}\left\langle\Psi^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi,\Xi_{i,j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{O}\left(Q_{R}^{p}\left|\log Q_{R}\right|\left\langle\Psi_{R}^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi_{R},\Xi_{i,j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right),\\
&c_{R,i,j}=-B_{*}\left\langle\Psi_{R}^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi_{R},\Xi_{i,j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{O}\left(Q_{R}^{p}\left|\log Q_{R}\right|\left\langle\Psi^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi,\Xi_{i,j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right)\end{aligned}\right.

and

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\varsigma_{i,j,l}=-B_{**}\left\langle\Psi^{p-1}w_{j},\Xi_{i,j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{O}\left(Q_{R}^{\frac{3p-1}{2}}\left|\log Q_{R}\right|\left\langle\Psi_{R}^{p-1}w_{R,j},\Xi_{i,j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right),\\
&\varsigma_{R,i,j,l}=-B_{**}\left\langle\Psi_{R}^{p-1}w_{R,j},\Xi_{i,j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{O}\left(Q_{R}^{\frac{3p-1}{2}}\left|\log Q_{R}\right|\left\langle\Psi^{p-1}w_{j},\Xi_{i,j}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right)\end{aligned}\right.

for all
1\leq l\leq d
, where
B_{*}=\left\|\Psi^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}
and
B_{**}=\left\|\Psi^{p-1}w_{d}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}
. Thus, by (
2.7
) and Lemma
4.3
, the oddness of
\partial_{t}\Psi
in
\mathbb{R}
and the oddness of
w_{d}
on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,

\displaystyle c_{1,1}\sim c_{R,1,1}\sim Q_{R}\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{l=1}^{d}\left(\left|\varsigma_{1,1,l}\right|+\left|\varsigma_{R,1,1,l}\right|\right)=0.

(9.11)

Similarly, we also have

\displaystyle|c_{1,2}|+|c_{R,1,2}|=0\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{l=1}^{d}\left(\left|\varsigma_{1,2,l}\right|+\left|\varsigma_{R,1,2,l}\right|\right)\lesssim Q_{R}.

(9.12)

Again, by (
2.7
) and Lemma
4.3
, the oddness of
\partial_{t}\Psi
in
\mathbb{R}
and the oddness of
w_{d}
on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, we have

\displaystyle c_{2,1}\sim c_{R,2,1}\sim Q_{R}^{p},\quad\sum_{l=1}^{d}\left(\left|\varsigma_{2,1,l}\right|+\left|\varsigma_{R,2,1,l}\right|\right)=0

(9.13)

and

\displaystyle c_{2,2}=c_{R,2,2}=0,\quad\sum_{l=1}^{d}\left(\left|\varsigma_{2,2,l}\right|+\left|\varsigma_{R,2,2,l}\right|\right)\sim 1

(9.14)

It follows that
\left\|\vartheta_{1,1}+\beta\vartheta_{1,2}+\beta^{2}\vartheta_{2,1}+\beta^{3}\vartheta_{2,2}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\sim\beta^{3}+Q_{R}
. On the other hand, by Lemmas
4.3
and
9.1
,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{N}_{\varrho_{*},rem,1}\right\|_{L^{2}}=o(\beta^{4}+Q_{R}).

Thus, we must have
\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\sim\beta^{3}+Q_{R}
.
∎

We decompose
\varrho=\widetilde{\alpha}\Psi+\widetilde{\alpha}_{R}\Psi_{R}+\widetilde{\varrho}^{\perp}
where
\widetilde{\alpha}
and
\widetilde{\alpha}_{R}
are chosen such that
\left\langle\Psi,\widetilde{\varrho}^{\perp}\right\rangle=0
and
\left\langle\Psi_{R},\widetilde{\varrho}^{\perp}\right\rangle=0
. It follows from Lemma
9.1
that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\alpha}\|\Psi\|^{2}+\mathcal{O}\left(Q_{R}\right)\widetilde{\alpha}_{R}=\left\langle\Xi_{1,1}+\beta\Xi_{1,2}+\beta^{2}\Xi_{2,1}+\beta^{3}\Xi_{2,2},\Psi\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\alpha}_{R}\|\Psi\|^{2}+\mathcal{O}\left(Q_{R}\right)\widetilde{\alpha}=\left\langle\Xi_{1,1}+\beta\Xi_{1,2}+\beta^{2}\Xi_{2,1}+\beta^{3}\Xi_{2,2},\Psi_{R}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}.

By (
9.2
) and (
9.3
),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\alpha}=(1+o(1))\widetilde{\alpha}=\left(\frac{p\int_{\mathcal{C}}\Psi^{p-1}d\mu}{\|\Psi\|^{2}}+o(1)\right)Q_{R}+\left(\frac{A_{p}\int_{\mathcal{C}}\Psi^{2p}d\mu}{\|\Psi\|^{2}}+o(1)\right)\beta^{2}.

(9.15)

Proposition 9.3
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
a<0
and
b=b_{FS}(a)
. If
Q_{R}\lesssim\beta^{3}
then
\left\|\beta\Phi_{R}+\widetilde{\varrho}^{\perp}\right\|\sim\left\|f\right\|_{H^{-1}}^{\frac{1}{3}}
as
\beta\to 0
.

Proof.

If
Q_{R}\lesssim\beta^{3}
then by Propositions
9.1
and
9.2
and (
9.15
), we have
\left\|\beta\Phi_{R}+\widetilde{\varrho}^{\perp}\right\|\sim\beta\sim\left\|f\right\|_{H^{-1}}^{\frac{1}{3}}
.
∎

For the sake of simplicity, we denote
\widetilde{v}=v+\varrho
. We shall decompose
\widetilde{v}
as in lemma
3.1
by considering the following variational problem:

\displaystyle\inf_{\overrightarrow{\alpha}_{\nu}\in\left(\mathbb{R}_{+}\right)^{2},\overrightarrow{s}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{\nu}}\left\|\widetilde{v}-\sum_{j=1}^{2}\alpha_{j}\Psi_{s_{j}}\right\|^{2}.

(9.16)

Clearly, as (
3.3
), the variational problem (
9.16
) has minimizers, say
(\widetilde{\alpha}^{*}_{1},\widetilde{\alpha}^{*}_{2},\widetilde{s}^{*}_{1},\widetilde{s}^{*}_{2})
, satisfying

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left|\widetilde{\alpha}^{*}_{j}-1\right|\to 0\quad\text{and}\quad\left|\widetilde{s}^{*}_{1}-\widetilde{s}^{*}_{2}\right|\to+\infty

(9.17)

as
R\to+\infty
and
\beta\to 0
.

Proposition 9.4
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
a<0
and
b=b_{FS}(a)
. Then for
R>0
sufficiently large and
\beta>0
sufficiently small, the variational problem (
9.16
) has a unique minimizer, say
(\widetilde{\alpha}^{*}_{1},\widetilde{\alpha}^{*}_{2},\widetilde{s}^{*}_{1},\widetilde{s}^{*}_{2})
, satisfying

\displaystyle\widetilde{s}^{*}_{1}=\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\beta^{2}+Q_{R}\right)^{2}\right),\quad\widetilde{s}^{*}_{2}=R+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\beta^{2}+Q_{R}\right)^{2}\right)

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\alpha}^{*}_{1}-1=\frac{\left\langle\rho,\Psi\right\rangle}{\|\Psi\|^{2}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\beta^{2}+Q_{R}\right)^{2}\right),\quad\widetilde{\alpha}^{*}_{2}-1=\frac{\left\langle\rho,\Psi_{R}\right\rangle}{\|\Psi\|^{2}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\beta^{2}+Q_{R}\right)^{2}\right).

Proof.

Since
(\widetilde{\alpha}^{*}_{1},\widetilde{\alpha}^{*}_{2},\widetilde{s}^{*}_{1},\widetilde{s}^{*}_{2})
is a minimizer of the variational problem (
9.16
) and
\Psi
and
\partial_{t}\Psi
are solutions of (
2.5
) and (
2.6
), respectively, we have

\displaystyle 0=\left\langle\widetilde{v}-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\widetilde{\alpha}^{*}_{i}\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{i}},\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{j}}\right\rangle=\left\langle\widetilde{v}-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\widetilde{\alpha}^{*}_{i}\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{i}},\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{j}}^{p}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

(9.18)

and

\displaystyle 0=\left\langle\widetilde{v}-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\widetilde{\alpha}^{*}_{i}\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{i}},\partial_{t}\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{j}}\right\rangle=\left\langle\widetilde{v}-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\widetilde{\alpha}^{*}_{i}\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{i}},p\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{j}}^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{j}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

(9.19)

for all
j=1,2
. By the oddness of
w_{d}
on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, the oddness of
\partial_{t}\Psi
in
\mathbb{R}
, (
9.17
) and (
9.19
), we have
\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\widetilde{\alpha}^{*}_{i}|\lesssim 1
and

\displaystyle\left\langle\Gamma_{R}+\varrho,p\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{j}}^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{j}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=\mathcal{O}\left(\left\langle\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{i}},\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{j}}^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{j}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right)=\mathcal{O}\left(Q_{R}^{\frac{\left|\widetilde{s}^{*}_{1}-\widetilde{s}^{*}_{2}\right|}{R}}\right).

Recall that
\varrho=\varrho_{1,1}+\beta\varrho_{1,2}+\beta^{2}\varrho_{2,1}+\beta^{3}\varrho_{2,2}
. Thus, by Lemma
9.1
and the fact that
\partial_{t}\Psi
solves (
2.6
), we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\varrho,p\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{j}}^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{j}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\Xi_{1,1}+\vartheta_{1,1}+\beta(\Xi_{1,2}+\vartheta_{1,2})+\beta^{2}(\Xi_{2,1}+\vartheta_{2,1}),\partial_{t}\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{j}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\left\langle\beta^{3}(\Xi_{2,2}+\vartheta_{2,2}),\partial_{t}\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{j}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\left\langle p\Gamma_{R}^{p-1}\varrho,\partial_{t}\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{j}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}.

It follows from (
2.7
), (
9.11
), (
9.12
), (
9.13
), (
9.14
), the oddness of
w_{d}
on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, the oddness of
\partial_{t}\Psi
in
\mathbb{R}
and Lemmas
4.3
and
9.1
that

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\varrho,\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{j}}^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi_{s_{j}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\langle\left|\Gamma_{R}^{p-1}\varrho\right|,\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{j}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\left\langle\left|\Xi_{1,1}+\beta^{2}\Psi_{R}^{p-2}w_{R,d}^{2}\chi_{\mathcal{B}_{R}}\right|,\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{j}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

(9.20)

\displaystyle+(c_{1,1}+c_{R,1,1})+\beta^{2}(c_{2,1}+c_{R,2,1})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\beta^{2}+Q_{R}.

On the other hand, for every
s_{j}\leq\frac{R}{2}
, by Lemma
4.3
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\Gamma_{R},\Psi_{s_{j}}^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi_{s_{j}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=\left\langle\Psi,\Psi_{s_{j}}^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi_{s_{j}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{O}\left(Q_{R}^{\frac{R-s_{j}}{R}}\right).

(9.21)

Note that by the evenness of
\Psi
and the oddness of
\partial_{t}\Psi
in
\mathbb{R}
,
\left\langle\Psi,\Psi_{s_{j}}^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi_{s_{j}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=0
has a uniquely nondegenerate solution
s_{j}=0
on
(-\infty,\frac{R}{2}]
. Thus, by (
9.20
), (
9.21
), the symmetry of
\Gamma_{R}
about
s=\frac{R}{2}
, for
R>0
sufficiently large, the solution of (
9.18
) and (
9.19
) must satify

\displaystyle\widetilde{s}^{*}_{1}=\mathcal{O}\left(\beta^{2}+Q_{R}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\widetilde{s}^{*}_{2}=R+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta^{2}+Q_{R}\right).

(9.22)

which, together with (
9.18
), implies that

\displaystyle\left\langle\Gamma_{R}+\varrho,\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{j}}^{p}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=\widetilde{\alpha}^{*}_{j}\|\Psi\|^{2}+\mathcal{O}\left(\left\langle\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{i}},\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{j}}^{p}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\right)=\widetilde{\alpha}^{*}_{j}\|\Psi\|^{2}+\mathcal{O}\left(Q_{R}\right)

for all
j=1,2
. Similar to (
9.20
) and (
9.21
), we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\Gamma_{R}+\varrho,\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{j}}^{p}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=\|\Psi\|^{2}+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta^{2}+Q_{R}\right).

Thus, we also have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\alpha}^{*}_{j}=1+\mathcal{O}\left(\beta^{2}+Q_{R}\right).

(9.23)

Now, by (
9.19
) once more, the oddness of
\partial_{t}^{3}\Psi
, the Taylor expansion and the orthogonal conditions of
\rho
given in (
9.4
), we have

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\widetilde{v}-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\widetilde{\alpha}^{*}_{i}\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{i}},\partial_{t}\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{j}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\Psi^{p},\partial_{t}\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{1}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\left\langle\Psi_{R}-\widetilde{\alpha}^{*}_{2}\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{2}},p\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{1}}^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{1}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\left\langle\varrho,p\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{1}}^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{1}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left\langle\Psi^{p},\partial_{t}^{2}\Psi\right\rangle_{L^{2}}\widetilde{s}^{*}_{1}+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\widetilde{s}^{*}_{1}\right)^{3}\right)+(1-\widetilde{\alpha}^{*}_{2})\left\langle\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{2}},p\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{1}}^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{1}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\left\langle\partial_{t}\Psi_{R},p\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{1}}^{p-1}\partial_{t}\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{1}}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}(\widetilde{s}^{*}_{2}-R)+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\widetilde{s}^{*}_{2}-R\right)^{2}+\|\varrho\|_{L^{\infty}}\widetilde{s}^{*}_{1}\right),

which, together with (
9.22
) and (
9.23
), implies that
\widetilde{s}^{*}_{1}=\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\beta^{2}+Q_{R}\right)^{2}\right)
. Similarly, we also have
\widetilde{s}^{*}_{2}=R+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\beta^{2}+Q_{R}\right)^{2}\right)
. Again, by (
9.18
), (
9.23
) and the Taylor expansion,

\displaystyle\left\langle\Gamma_{R}+\varrho,\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{1}}^{p}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\alpha}^{*}_{1}\|\Psi\|^{2}+\left\langle\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{2}},\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{1}}^{p}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\beta^{2}+Q_{R}\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\alpha}^{*}_{1}\|\Psi\|^{2}+\left\langle\Psi_{R},\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{1}}^{p}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\beta^{2}+Q_{R}\right)^{2}\right),

which, together with

\displaystyle\left\langle\Gamma_{R}+\varrho,\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{1}}^{p}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=\|\Psi\|^{2}+\left\langle\Psi_{R},\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{1}}^{p}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}+\left\langle\rho,\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{1}}^{p}\right\rangle_{L^{2}},

implies that
\widetilde{\alpha}^{*}_{1}-1=\frac{\left\langle\varrho,\Psi\right\rangle}{\|\Psi\|^{2}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\beta^{2}+Q_{R}\right)^{2}\right)
.
Similarly, we also have
\widetilde{\alpha}^{*}_{2}-1=\frac{\left\langle\varrho,\Psi_{R}\right\rangle}{\|\Psi\|^{2}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\beta^{2}+Q_{R}\right)^{2}\right)
.
∎

Let
\widetilde{v}_{\pm}=\max\{\pm\widetilde{v},0\}
. Then
\widetilde{v}=\widetilde{v}_{+}-\widetilde{v}_{-}
and by (
9.7
),

\displaystyle-\Delta_{\theta}\widetilde{v}_{+}-\partial_{t}^{2}\widetilde{v}_{+}+\Lambda_{FS}\widetilde{v}_{+}-\widetilde{v}_{+}^{p}=f+\mathcal{G}(\widetilde{v}_{-}):=f_{\widetilde{v}_{+}},

(9.24)

where
\mathcal{G}(\widetilde{v}_{-})=-\Delta_{\theta}\widetilde{v}_{-}-\partial_{t}^{2}\widetilde{v}_{-}+\Lambda_{FS}\widetilde{v}_{-}-\widetilde{v}_{-}^{p}
.

Proof of
(b)
of Theorem
1.3
:

Recall that we have the decomposition

\displaystyle\widetilde{v}=v+\widetilde{\alpha}\Psi+\widetilde{\alpha}_{R}\Psi_{R}+\widetilde{\varrho}^{\perp}

(9.25)

in
H^{1}(\mathcal{C})
,
where by the orthogonal conditions of
\widetilde{\varrho}^{\perp}
and (
9.15
),

\displaystyle\left\langle\varrho,\Psi\right\rangle=\widetilde{\alpha}\|\Psi\|^{2}+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\beta^{2}+Q_{R}\right)^{2}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\left\langle\varrho,\Psi_{R}\right\rangle=\widetilde{\alpha}_{R}\|\Psi\|^{2}+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\beta^{2}+Q_{R}\right)^{2}\right).

It follows from Proposition
9.4
that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\alpha}^{*}_{1}=1+\widetilde{\alpha}+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\beta^{2}+Q_{R}\right)^{2}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\widetilde{\alpha}^{*}_{2}=1+\widetilde{\alpha}_{R}+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\beta^{2}+Q_{R}\right)^{2}\right),

which, together with Proposition
9.4
once more and the Taylor expansion, implies that

\displaystyle\widetilde{v}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{2}\widetilde{\alpha}^{*}_{j}\Psi_{\widetilde{s}^{*}_{j}}+\widetilde{\varrho}

(9.26)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma_{R}+\widetilde{\alpha}\Psi+\widetilde{\alpha}_{R}\Psi_{R}+\widetilde{\varrho}+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\beta^{2}+Q_{R}\right)^{2}\right)

in
H^{1}(\mathcal{C})
. By (
9.25
) and (
9.26
), we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\varrho}=\beta\Phi_{R}+\widetilde{\varrho}^{\perp}+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\beta^{2}+Q_{R}\right)^{2}\right).

Thus, by (
9.16
), Propositions
9.3
and
9.4
, we have

\displaystyle\inf_{\overrightarrow{\alpha}_{\nu}\in\left(\mathbb{R}_{+}\right)^{2},\overrightarrow{s}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{\nu}}\left\|\widetilde{v}-\sum_{j=1}^{2}\alpha_{j}\Psi_{s_{j}}\right\|\sim\|f\|_{H^{-1}}^{\frac{1}{3}}.

(9.27)

By Lemma
9.1
, we know that
|\beta\Phi_{R}+\varrho_{1,1}+\beta\varrho_{1,2}|\lesssim\Gamma_{R}
in
\mathcal{C}
for sufficiently small
\beta
and sufficiently large
R
. Thus,
0\leq\widetilde{v}_{-}\leq\beta^{2}\varrho_{2,1}+\beta^{3}\varrho_{2,2}|
in
\mathcal{C}
. It follows from (
9.7
), Lemma
9.1
and Proposition
9.2
that

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{v}_{-}\|^{2}\lesssim\left\langle f,\widetilde{v}_{-}\right\rangle_{L^{2}}=\mathcal{O}\left(\left(\beta^{2}+Q_{R}\right)^{2}\right),

which, together with (
9.24
) and (
9.27
), implies that
\widetilde{v}_{+}
is the desired functions.

\Box

Remark 9.1
.

The optimal example of Theorem
1.3
in this section, given by
\widetilde{v}=v+\varrho
, precisely describes the relation between
\|f\|_{H^{-1}}
and

\displaystyle\inf_{\overrightarrow{\alpha}_{\nu}\in\left(\mathbb{R}_{+}\right)^{2},\overrightarrow{s}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{\nu}}\left\|\widetilde{v}-\sum_{j=1}^{2}\alpha_{j}\Psi_{s_{j}}\right\|.

Indeed, we have
\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\sim\beta^{3}+Q_{R}
and

\displaystyle\inf_{\overrightarrow{\alpha}_{\nu}\in\left(\mathbb{R}_{+}\right)^{2},\overrightarrow{s}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{\nu}}\left\|\widetilde{v}-\sum_{j=1}^{2}\alpha_{j}\Psi_{s_{j}}\right\|\sim\beta+\left\{\begin{aligned} &Q_{R},\quad p>2,\\
&Q_{R}\left|\log Q_{R}\right|,\quad p=2,\\
&Q_{R}^{\frac{p}{2}},\quad 1<p<2.\end{aligned}\right.

If the interaction of two bubbles is much smaller than their projections on nontrivial kernel, that is,
\beta^{3}\gtrsim Q_{R}
, then we have

\displaystyle\inf_{\overrightarrow{\alpha}_{\nu}\in\left(\mathbb{R}_{+}\right)^{2},\overrightarrow{s}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{\nu}}\left\|\widetilde{v}-\sum_{j=1}^{2}\alpha_{j}\Psi_{s_{j}}\right\|\sim\|f\|_{H^{-1}}^{\frac{1}{3}}.

If the interaction of two bubbles is much large than their projections on nontrivial kernel, that is,

\displaystyle\beta\lesssim\left\{\begin{aligned} &Q_{R},\quad p>2,\\
&Q_{R}\left|\log Q_{R}\right|,\quad p=2,\\
&Q_{R}^{\frac{p}{2}},\quad 1<p<2,\end{aligned}\right.

then we have

\displaystyle\inf_{\overrightarrow{\alpha}_{\nu}\in\left(\mathbb{R}_{+}\right)^{2},\overrightarrow{s}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{\nu}}\left\|\widetilde{v}-\sum_{j=1}^{2}\alpha_{j}\Psi_{s_{j}}\right\|\sim\left\{\begin{aligned} &\|f\|_{H^{-1}},\quad p>2,\\
&\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\left|\log\|f\|_{H^{-1}}\right|,\quad p=2,\\
&\|f\|_{H^{-1}}^{\frac{p}{2}},\quad 1<p<2.\end{aligned}\right.

If the interaction of two bubbles is somehow comparable with their projections on nontrivial kernel, that is
\beta^{3}\lesssim Q_{R}
and

\displaystyle\beta\gtrsim\left\{\begin{aligned} &Q_{R},\quad p>2,\\
&Q_{R}\left|\log Q_{R}\right|,\quad p=2,\\
&Q_{R}^{\frac{p}{2}},\quad 1<p<2,\end{aligned}\right.

then we have

\displaystyle\inf_{\overrightarrow{\alpha}_{\nu}\in\left(\mathbb{R}_{+}\right)^{2},\overrightarrow{s}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{\nu}}\left\|\widetilde{v}-\sum_{j=1}^{2}\alpha_{j}\Psi_{s_{j}}\right\|\sim

\displaystyle\|f\|_{H^{-1}}^{t}

with

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{1}{3}\geq t\leq 1,\quad p>2,\\
&\frac{1}{3}\leq t\leq 1+o(1),\quad p=2,\\
&\frac{1}{3}\leq t\leq\frac{p}{2},\quad 1<p<2.\end{aligned}\right.
